-4

Table 2:
List of zero-mode number
n^{0}_{\phi}
for different fluctuations around the 4D black hole backgrounds. The values of the scaling dimension parameter
\beta_{\phi}
are mentioned in the corresponding columns. The negative values for the AdS
4
and extremal near-horizon backgrounds arise from the renormalized finite part of the zero-mode integral (
12
)
Banerjee:2011oo
;
Banerjee:2011pp
;
Sen:2012rr
;
Sen:2012qq
;
Bhattacharyya:2012ye
;
Liu:2017vbl
.

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{\text{zm}}=-(3+\mathbb{K})+3\delta_{\text{non-ext}}+2\mathbb{N}_{\text{BPS}},

(37)

where only the metric and spin-3/2 gravitino fluctuations contribute. For an extremal black bole (
\beta\to\infty
), the related analysis fully relies on the near-horizon geometry and is controlled by a dominant AdS
2
part which provides
-3
zero-modes corresponding to unbroken translational symmetry (i.e., SL(
2,\mathbb{R}
) symmetry of AdS
2
spaces). However, this contribution will be eliminated for non-extremal black holes where one needs to shift from the near-horizon to full geometry analysis by incorporating a
3\delta_{\text{non-ext}}
contribution to account for the additional IR volume integration due to a finite
\beta
(inverse temperature). Thus,
\delta_{\text{non-ext}}
is 1 for non-extremal black holes, otherwise 0. Indeed the same fact also holds for AdS
4
black holes, which generally admit a 2-form zero-mode that vanishes when embedded in 4D theories
Bhattacharyya:2012ye
;
Liu:2017vbl
;
Camporesi:1994ye
. In addition, there will be a contribution of
-K
zero-modes associated with the rotational symmetries of both extremal and non-extremal black holes, which are induced due to change in the ensemble from grand canonical to microcanonical in step (
4
) by fixing black hole mass
M
, charge
\vec{Q}
, and angular momentum
\vec{J}
. It is found that
K
is 3 for spherically symmetric non-rotating (
J_{3}=\vec{J^{2}}=0
) backgrounds, otherwise 1 for any rotating black hole. Apart from these zero modes of the metric fluctuation (or graviton), the spin-3/2 gravitino fluctuation around a BPS black hole gives rise to a
2\mathbb{N}_{\text{BPS}}
contribution, where
\mathbb{N}_{\text{BPS}}=4
corresponds to the number of preserved supersymmetry (i.e., the number of generators of PSU(1, 1|2) near-horizon symmetry) of BPS solutions in supergravity. However, around the backgrounds of any non-supersymmetric black hole, there will be no spin-3/2 zero-modes, i.e.,
\mathbb{N}_{\text{BPS}}=0
. In this paper, we will directly use the formula (
37
) or the data recorded in
Table

3
to obtain the zero-mode contributions of black holes embedded into four-dimensional EMD and EMD-AdS theories.

3
Heat kernel coefficients in EMD and EMD-AdS theories

This section aims to quantize the four-dimensional Einstein-Maxwell-dilaton (EMD) gravity models by fluctuating all the content and then analyze the quadratic fluctuation spectrum to calculate the Seeley-DeWitt coefficients for finding the logarithmic correction to the entropy of embedded black holes. Throughout, we will consider a generic
U(1)
-charged EMD theory with a negative cosmological constant
\Lambda
, i.e., evolving in AdS
4
space of boundary
\ell
so that
\Lambda=-\dfrac{3}{\ell^{2}}
. In the end, the account of flat-space limit
\ell\to\infty
will serve the similar heat kernel results for the simple EMD theory with a vanishing cosmological constant (i.e.,
\Lambda=0
).

The dynamics of a general four-dimensional
U(1)
-charged EMD model is given by the action,
12
12

12

The strength gravitational interaction is set as
8\pi G_{4}=\frac{1}{2}
.

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}[g_{\mu\nu},A_{\mu},\Phi]=\int\mathrm{d}^{4}x\sqrt{\det g}\left(\mathcal{R}-2\Lambda-2D_{\mu}\Phi D^{\mu}\Phi-f(\Phi){F}_{\mu\nu}{F}^{\mu\nu}\right),f(\Phi)=e^{-2\kappa\Phi},

(38)

where
\mathcal{R}=g^{\mu\nu}\mathcal{R}^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}{\alpha}{\nu}}}
is the Ricci curvature scalar related to the spacetime metric
g_{\mu\nu}
,
{F}_{\mu\nu}={D_{[\mu}A_{\nu]}}
is the 2-form field strength of the
U(1)
Maxwell field
A_{\mu}
and
\Phi
is a dilaton field that is minimally coupled to Einstein’s gravity but non-minimally coupled to the Maxwell sector. The “Maxwell-Dilaton” interaction is controlled by an exponential coupling function
f(\Phi)=e^{-2\kappa\Phi}
with the dilaton coupling constant
\kappa
. As already mentioned, the EMD families of theories are of special interest in string theory and appear as a universal toy model of the low-energy effective theories of superstring or supergravities. Generally, the dilaton coupling function
f(\Phi)
is determined by the tree-level string interactions where the string couplings are related via the vacuum expectation value of dilaton,
g_{s}=e^{\left\langle\Phi\right\rangle}
. The dilaton coupling constant
\kappa
is fixed via the dimensional reduction or compactification of consistent string theories in 4D. It is found that the specific values
\kappa=\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}
,
\kappa=1
and
\kappa=\sqrt{3}
particularly embed the EMD models (
38
) into various supergravity and Kaluza-Klein theories
Gibbons:1982ih
;
Gibbons:1987ps
;
Garfinkle:1990qj
;
Horowitz:1991cd
;
Kallosh:1992ii
;
Khuri:1995xk
;
Gao:2004tv
;
Becker:2006dvp
;
Cvetic:2014vsa
;
GoulartSantos:2017dun
;
Guo:2021zed
;
Zhang:2021edm
;
Myung:2020dqt
. Note that the exponential nature of the dilaton coupling function
f(\Phi)
is so that its expansion can mimic the string loop expansion. In this paper, we will also execute a similar expansion while fluctuating the EMD action (
38
) around classical backgrounds to find the one-loop quantum correction.

3.1
Equations of motion and embedding of Einstein-Maxwell backgrounds

Evolution of the action (
38
) with respect to the metric, Maxwell field, and dilaton steams out the EMD equations of motion for an arbitrary classical background
(\bar{g}_{\mu\nu},\bar{A}_{\mu},\bar{\Phi})
. The gravitational field equations are obtained as

\displaystyle R_{\mu\nu}-\frac{1}{2}\bar{g}_{\mu\nu}R+\Lambda\bar{g}_{\mu\nu}=T_{\mu\nu}^{\mathrm{(dilaton)}}+T_{\mu\nu}^{U(1)},

(39a)

with the following dilaton and
U(1)
or Maxwell parts of the total stress-energy tensor

\displaystyle T_{\mu\nu}^{\mathrm{(dilaton)}}

\displaystyle=2\left(D_{\mu}\bar{\Phi}D_{\nu}\bar{\Phi}-\frac{1}{2}\bar{g}_{\mu\nu}D_{\rho}\bar{\Phi}D^{\rho}\bar{\Phi}\right),

(39b)

\displaystyle T_{\mu\nu}^{U(1)}

\displaystyle=e^{-2\kappa\bar{\Phi}}T_{\mu\nu}^{\mathrm{(Maxwell)}}=2e^{-2\kappa\bar{\Phi}}\left(\bar{F}_{\mu\rho}\bar{F}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\rho}}}_{{{\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}-\frac{1}{4}\bar{g}_{\mu\nu}\bar{F}_{\rho\sigma}\bar{F}^{\rho\sigma}\right),

(39c)

where
\bar{F}_{\mu\nu}={\partial_{[\mu}\bar{A}_{\nu]}}
is the background Maxwell field strength. The Maxwell and dilaton evolution equations take the forms,

\displaystyle D_{\mu}\left(e^{-2\kappa\bar{\Phi}}\bar{F}^{\mu\nu}\right)=0,\thinspace D_{[\mu}\bar{F}_{\rho\sigma]}=0,

(40)

\displaystyle D_{\mu}D^{\mu}\bar{\Phi}+\frac{1}{2}\kappa e^{-2\kappa\bar{\Phi}}\bar{F}_{\mu\nu}\bar{F}^{\mu\nu}=0.

(41)

The above equations of motion are invariant under both the electromagnetic duality rotation and dilaton transformation,

\displaystyle\left\{Q\to P,\thinspace P\to Q\right\}\kern 5.0pt\text{and}\kern 5.0ptf(\bar{\Phi})\to\dfrac{1}{f(\bar{\Phi})},

where
Q
and
P
are respectively the electric and magnetic charges related to the background Maxwell field
\bar{A}_{\mu}
. Indeed, the EMD background solutions remain invariant for the change
(\kappa,\bar{\Phi})\to(-\kappa,-\bar{\Phi})
, where any alteration of the signature of
\kappa
must imply a flip in the signature of
\bar{\Phi}
and vice-versa.

The EMD theory (
38
) is a natural but nontrivial generalization of the standard Einstein-Maxwell (EM) of gravity that is minimally coupled to the Maxwell sector,

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}[g_{\mu\nu},A_{\mu}]=\int\mathrm{d}^{4}x\sqrt{\det g}\left(\mathcal{R}-2\Lambda-{F}_{\mu\nu}{F}^{\mu\nu}\right),

(42)

where the background solutions are well-known Kerr-Newman, Kerr, Reissner-Nordström and Schwarzschild black holes satisfying the following equations of motion

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}R_{\mu\nu}-\bar{g}_{\mu\nu}\Lambda=2\bar{F}_{\mu\rho}\bar{F}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\rho}}}_{{{\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}-\frac{1}{2}\bar{g}_{\mu\nu}\bar{F}_{\rho\sigma}\bar{F}^{\rho\sigma},\thinspace R=4\Lambda,\\
D_{\mu}\bar{F}^{\mu\nu}=0,\thinspace D_{[\mu}\bar{F}_{\rho\sigma]}=0.\end{gathered}

Generally, the EM system can not be obtained as a consistent truncation of EMD theories due to the presence of the Maxwell-dilaton coupling term
e^{-2\kappa\Phi}{F}_{\mu\nu}{F}^{\mu\nu}
in the action (
38
). But, it is possible to recover the EM backgrounds from the EMD equations of motion (
39
)-(
41
) for two specific cases:

(i)

\bar{\Phi}=\mathrm{constant}
and
\kappa=0
,

(ii)

\bar{\Phi}=\mathrm{constant}
and
\bar{F}_{\mu\nu}\bar{F}^{\mu\nu}=0
.
13
13

13

Note that the constant dilaton explicitly can not recover an EM system from the EMD theory but reduces it into the Brans-Dicke-Maxwell system with a Brans-Dicke coupling constant
\omega=-1

Cai:1996pj
.

The former case is nothing but a natural limit describing an EM theory minimally coupled to the kinetic part of a constant scalar field (not necessarily a dilaton). In contrast, the latter case can embed the EM backgrounds into the EMD theory and is of particular interest to this paper. For
\kappa\neq 0
, it is possible to interpret
\bar{\Phi}=0
as a solution of EMD equations if
\bar{F}_{\mu\nu}\bar{F}^{\mu\nu}=0
. This is the particular EM embedding we will restrict ourselves, which includes an equal-charged Reissner-Nordström (RN), but not in general an equal-charged Kerr-Newman black hole. In other words, a non-rotating dyonic black hole with equal electric and magnetic charges always satisfies the EM embedding condition,

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}Q=P,\thinspace J=0\longrightarrow\bar{F}^{\text{(EMD)}}_{\mu\nu}\bar{F}^{\text{(EMD)}\mu\nu}=0,\thinspace\bar{\Phi}^{\text{(EMD)}}=0.\end{gathered}

The above condition is naturally satisfied by the uncharged Schwarzschild and Kerr backgrounds and is also common for both the asymptotically flat and AdS limits of the embedded black holes. The whole fact can be checked by setting
Q=P
and
(Q,P)=0
in the relation (
4.2
) for both
J=0
and
J\neq 0
. Here we want to emphasize an important remark – a genuine RN solution of the EM theory (
42
) can contain any combination of charges
(Q,P)
with an effective value,
Q_{e}=\sqrt{Q^{2}+P^{2}}
, which is different from the RN black holes embedded in the EMD theory (
38
) that must support
Q=P
. In summary, the constraint (
3.1
) on EMD background effectively decouples the non-minimal Maxwell-Dilaton interaction from the dilaton equation (
41
) and thus guarantees that the scalar-free or bald Reissner-Nordström, Kerr and Schwarzschild as solutions of the EMD theory satisfying the EM background equations (
3.1
). This entire setup of EM embedding will essentially boost the prospect of microscopic visibility of the black holes when we compute logarithmic corrections to their entropy by fluctuating the EMD content. In this line, one should not worry about the setting of vanishing dilaton background (i.e.,
\bar{\Phi}=0
) since, while quantizing the EMD theory, there will always be the Maxwell-dilaton interactions and related contributions via the perturbative expansion of the coupling function
f(\Phi)
for any small dilaton fluctuation
\tilde{\Phi}
around
\bar{\Phi}=0)
,

\displaystyle f(\bar{\Phi}+\tilde{\Phi})\big|_{\bar{\Phi}=0}\approx f(\bar{\Phi})+\tilde{\Phi}\frac{\mathrm{d}f(\Phi)}{\mathrm{d}\Phi}\bigg|_{\bar{\Phi}=0}+\frac{\tilde{\Phi}^{2}}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}^{2}f(\Phi)}{\mathrm{d}\Phi^{2}}\bigg|_{\bar{\Phi}=0}+\cdots\cdotp.

(46)

3.2
Quadratic fluctuations and computation of Seeley-DeWitt coefficients

To determine quantum entropy corrections for black holes in EMD theory, one needs to analyze the spectrum of quadratic fluctuations of action (
38
). We aim to do this via computing the Seeley-DeWitt coefficients up to the first three orders by fluctuating the field content of EMD theory around the EM background, where dilaton is assumed to vanish at the background. In particular, we consider the following fluctuations

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}g_{\mu\nu}=\bar{g}_{\mu\nu}+\sqrt{2}h_{\mu\nu},\kern 5.0ptA_{\mu}=\bar{A}_{\mu}+\frac{1}{2}a_{\mu},\kern 5.0pt\Phi=\cancelto{0}{\bar{\Phi}}+\tilde{\Phi},\\
F_{\mu\nu}=\bar{F}_{\mu\nu}+\frac{1}{2}f_{\mu\nu},\kern 5.0pt{f}_{\mu\nu}={\partial_{[\mu}a_{\nu]}}={D_{[\mu}a_{\nu]}},\end{gathered}

where
h_{\mu\nu}
and
a_{\mu}
are the thermal fluctuations of metric (i.e., graviton) and Maxwell field (i.e., graviphoton) with the field strength
{f}_{\mu\nu}
. Inside the present setup, dilaton actually behaves as its own fluctuation while sharing the common EM background
(\bar{g}_{\mu\nu},\bar{A}_{\mu})
with graviton and graviphoton. This will, in turn, induce several non-minimal couplings between the dilaton, graviton and graviphoton via the background field strength
\bar{F}_{\mu\nu}
. The normalization factors of the graviton and graviphoton in (
3.2
) are set according to the convention of
Bhattacharyya:2012ss
, which will be essential in the following heat kernel treatment. With the fluctuations (
3.2
) and (
46
), we obtain the quadratic-order variation of the action (
38
) up to a total derivative and express the minimal blocks of “cosmological Einstein gravity” and “Maxwell-dilaton” as

\displaystyle\delta^{2}\left(\sqrt{\det g}\,(\mathcal{R}-2\Lambda)\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{\det\bar{g}}\bigg[h_{\mu\nu}D_{\rho}D^{\rho}h^{\mu\nu}-h^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}D_{\rho}D^{\rho}h^{{{\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}-2h^{\nu\rho}D_{\mu}D_{\nu}h^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\rho}}}

\displaystyle\qquad+2h^{\mu\nu}D_{\mu}D_{\nu}h^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}+2{R}_{\mu\nu}\big(2h^{\mu\rho}h^{{{\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\rho}}}-h^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}h^{\mu\nu}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad-\left(R-2\Lambda\right)h_{\mu\nu}h^{\mu\nu}+\bigg(\frac{R}{2}-\Lambda\bigg)(h^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}})^{2}\bigg],

(49a)

\displaystyle-\delta^{2}\left(\sqrt{\det g}\,(2D_{\mu}\Phi D^{\mu}\Phi+f(\Phi){F}_{\mu\nu}{F}^{\mu\nu})\right)=\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{\det\bar{g}}\bigg[4\Phi D_{\rho}D^{\rho}\Phi-\frac{1}{2}f_{\mu\nu}f^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle\hskip 137.31255pt-4\bar{F}_{\mu\nu}\bar{F}_{\alpha\beta}h^{\mu\alpha}h^{\nu\beta}-8\bar{F}_{\mu\nu}\bar{F}^{\mu\alpha}h^{\nu\beta}h_{\alpha\beta}+4\bar{F}_{\mu\nu}\bar{F}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}_{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}h^{{{\rho}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\rho}}}h^{\mu\alpha}

\displaystyle\hskip 137.31255pt+\bar{F}_{\mu\nu}\bar{F}^{\mu\nu}\Big(h_{\alpha\beta}h^{\alpha\beta}-\frac{1}{2}(h^{{{\rho}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\rho}}})^{2}\Big)-4\kappa^{2}\bar{F}_{\mu\nu}\bar{F}^{\mu\nu}\Phi^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 137.31255pt-8\sqrt{2}\kappa\bar{F}_{\mu\nu}\bar{F}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}_{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\Phi h^{\mu\alpha}+2\sqrt{2}\kappa\bar{F}_{\mu\nu}\bar{F}^{\mu\nu}\Phi h^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 137.31255pt+4\kappa\bar{F}_{\mu\nu}\Phi f^{\mu\nu}+4\sqrt{2}\bar{F}_{\mu\nu}h^{\mu\alpha}f^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}_{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}-\sqrt{2}\bar{F}_{\mu\nu}h^{{{\rho}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\rho}}}f^{\mu\nu}\bigg].

(49b)

Before proceeding further, we find it necessary to gauge-fix the theory. One can execute the gauge-fixing exactly the same way as in the asymptotically-flat case (i.e.,
\Lambda=0
)
Bhattacharyya:2012ss
since incorporating a cosmological term into the action never affects the gauge invariance of a concerned theory. The most convenient choice is to set the harmonic gauge
D_{\mu}h^{\mu\rho}-\frac{1}{2}D^{\rho}h^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}=0
and Lorentz gauge
D_{\mu}a^{\mu}=0
via adding the gauge-fixing term,

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{\text{gauge}}=-\int\mathrm{d}^{4}x\sqrt{\det\bar{g}}\left[\left(D_{\mu}h^{\mu\rho}-\frac{1}{2}D^{\rho}h^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}\right)\left(D^{\nu}h_{\nu\rho}-\frac{1}{2}D_{\rho}h^{{{\beta}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}\right)+\frac{1}{2}(D_{\mu}a^{\mu})^{2}\right],

(50)

followed by the compensating ghost term
Banerjee:2011oo
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{S}_{\text{ghost}}[b_{\mu},c_{\mu},b,c]&=\int\mathrm{d}^{4}x\sqrt{\det\bar{g}}\big[b_{\mu}D_{\rho}D^{\rho}c^{\mu}+bD_{\rho}D^{\rho}c+b_{\mu}R^{\mu\nu}c_{\nu}-2b\bar{F}^{\rho\nu}D_{\rho}c_{\nu}\big],\end{split}

(51)

where the vector fields
(b_{\mu},c_{\mu})
are diffeomorphism ghosts related to the graviton and the scalar fields
(b,c)
are ghosts induced due to the gauge invariance of graviphoton. These ghost fields are all minimally-coupled and never interact with any of the graviton, graviphoton, and dilaton fluctuations. So we have the freedom to evaluate their contribution separately (see
eq.

75
). Then, without accounting for the ghosts, the gauge fixed quadratic fluctuated EMD action is obtained as

\displaystyle{\delta^{2}\mathcal{S}}[h_{\mu\nu},a_{\mu},\Phi]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int\mathrm{d}^{4}x\sqrt{\det\bar{g}}\bigg[h_{\mu\nu}D_{\rho}D^{\rho}h^{\mu\nu}-\frac{1}{2}h^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}D_{\rho}D^{\rho}h^{{{\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}+a_{\mu}D_{\rho}D^{\rho}a^{\mu}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+4\Phi D_{\rho}D^{\rho}\Phi+2R_{\mu\alpha\nu\beta}h^{\mu\nu}h^{\alpha\beta}-2R_{\mu\nu}h^{\mu\rho}h^{{{\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\rho}}}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad-4\bar{F}_{\mu\nu}\bar{F}_{\alpha\beta}h^{\mu\alpha}h^{\nu\beta}+2\Lambda\Big(h_{\mu\nu}h^{\mu\nu}-\frac{1}{2}(h^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}})^{2}\Big)-a_{\mu}R^{\mu\nu}a_{\nu}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad-4\sqrt{2}\kappa R_{\mu\alpha}\Phi h^{\mu\alpha}+4\sqrt{2}\kappa\Lambda\Phi h^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}+8\kappa\Phi\bar{F}^{\rho\mu}D_{\rho}a_{\mu}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+2\sqrt{2}h_{\mu\nu}\big(2\bar{g}^{\nu\rho}\bar{F}^{\mu\alpha}-2\bar{g}^{\alpha\nu}\bar{F}^{\mu\rho}-\bar{g}^{\mu\nu}\bar{F}^{\rho\alpha}\big)(D_{\rho}a_{\alpha})\bigg],

(52)

where the EM embedding condition (
3.1
) and background equations of motion (
3.1
) are utilized to achieve the above intricate form. Now, we are in a position to carry out the heat kernel method depicted in
section

2.3.1
. But before that, we must resolve a few issues to extract the kinetic operator
\mathcal{H}
out of the quadratic action (
3.2
) in the desired Laplace-type form (
30
). The kinetic terms of each fluctuation in (
3.2
) must be adjusted into the same state of normalization. But notice that the kinetic part of graviton consists of two terms with a total of ten degrees of freedom. In particular, the appearance of an additional kinetic term associated with the graviton trace, i.e.,
h^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}D_{\rho}D^{\rho}h^{{{\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}
is problematic. As a resolution, there exists a convenient method of transforming the explicit graviton kinetic part
h_{\mu\nu}D_{\rho}D^{\rho}h^{\mu\nu}
into the appropriate irreducible representation of
SL(2,\mathbb{C})

Christensen:1979iy
;
Christensen:1978md
;
Gibbons:1978ji
. In this progress, we need to split the graviton
h_{\mu\nu}
into its trace-free (as well as symmetric) component
\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}
and trace component
h
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}&=h_{\mu\nu}-\frac{1}{4}\bar{g}_{\mu\nu}h,\\
h&=h^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}=\bar{g}^{\mu\nu}h_{\mu\nu},\end{split}

(53)

and then express the fluctuated action as

\displaystyle{\delta^{2}\mathcal{S}}[h_{\mu\nu},a_{\mu},\Phi]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int\mathrm{d}^{4}x\sqrt{\det\bar{g}}\bigg[I^{\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}\hat{h}_{\alpha\beta}}\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}D_{\rho}D^{\rho}\hat{h}_{\alpha\beta}-\frac{1}{4}hD_{\rho}D^{\rho}h

\displaystyle\qquad+a_{\mu}D_{\rho}D^{\rho}a^{\mu}+\Phi D_{\rho}D^{\rho}\Phi+2R^{\mu\alpha\nu\beta}\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}\hat{h}_{\alpha\beta}-2R^{\mu\nu}\hat{h}_{\mu\rho}\hat{h}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\rho}}}_{{{\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}

\displaystyle\qquad-4\bar{F}^{\mu\nu}\bar{F}^{\alpha\beta}\hat{h}_{\mu\alpha}\hat{h}_{\nu\beta}-2\bar{F}^{\mu\alpha}\bar{F}^{{{\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}h+2\Lambda\Big(\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}\hat{h}^{\mu\nu}-\frac{1}{4}h^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad-a_{\mu}R^{\mu\nu}a_{\nu}-2\sqrt{2}\kappa R^{\mu\nu}\Phi\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}+4\kappa\Phi\bar{F}^{\rho\mu}D_{\rho}a_{\mu}

\displaystyle\qquad+4\sqrt{2}\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}\big(\bar{g}^{\nu\rho}\bar{F}^{\mu\alpha}-\bar{g}^{\alpha\nu}\bar{F}^{\mu\rho}\big)(D_{\rho}a_{\alpha})\bigg],

(54)

where the dilaton has been rescaled as
\Phi\to\dfrac{1}{2}\Phi
. Notice that the kinetic term of the trace-free graviton is now modulated via a newly introduced operator
I^{\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}\hat{h}_{\alpha\beta}}
. This operator is a projection operator onto the trace-free graviton, which should contain
10-1=9
independent off-shell degrees of freedom and can be structured via the following combination of background metrices,

\displaystyle I^{\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}\hat{h}_{\alpha\beta}}=\frac{1}{2}\left(\bar{g}^{\mu\alpha}\bar{g}^{\nu\beta}+\bar{g}^{\mu\beta}\bar{g}^{\nu\alpha}-\frac{1}{2}\bar{g}^{\mu\nu}\bar{g}^{\alpha\beta}\right).

(55)

The covariant partner
I_{\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}\hat{h}_{\alpha\beta}}
also follows exactly a similar form. The projection operator form (
55
) actually acts as an effective background metric (called DeWitt metric) to contract the indices of arbitrary matrices
\mathbb{M}
acting on the trace-free graviton fluctuations,

\displaystyle\phi_{m}\mathbb{M}^{m}_{n}\phi^{n}=\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}{\mathbb{M}}^{\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}\hat{h}_{\alpha\beta}}\hat{h}_{\alpha\beta},

(56)

and define their (as well as of its own) traces as

\displaystyle\begin{split}\text{Tr}(\mathbb{M})&=\mathbb{M}^{{{\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}}\mathchoice{\makebox[11.42857pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.42857pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.89342pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[9.89342pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[11.42857pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.42857pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.89342pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[9.89342pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}}}}={I}_{\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}\hat{h}_{\alpha\beta}}{\mathbb{M}}^{\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}\hat{h}_{\alpha\beta}},\\
\text{Tr}(\mathbb{M}^{2})&=\mathbb{M}^{{{\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}}\mathchoice{\makebox[11.96167pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.96167pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[10.27422pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[10.27422pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[11.42857pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.42857pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.89342pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[9.89342pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\hat{h}_{\alpha\beta}}}}\mathbb{M}^{{{\hat{h}_{\alpha\beta}}\mathchoice{\makebox[11.42857pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.42857pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.89342pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[9.89342pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[11.96167pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.96167pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[10.27422pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[10.27422pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}}}}={I}_{\hat{h}_{\alpha\beta}\hat{h}_{\rho\sigma}}{I}_{\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}\hat{h}_{\gamma\delta}}{\mathbb{M}}^{\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}\hat{h}_{\rho\sigma}}{\mathbb{M}}^{\hat{h}_{\alpha\beta}\hat{h}_{\gamma\delta}}.\end{split}

(57)

The quadratic action form (
3.2
) still suffers the “conformal factor problem” due to the negative signature of graviton trace kinetic term, making the Euclidean one-loop path integral (
8
) unbounded, divergent and ill-defined. However, this problem can be resolved by performing the standard treatment
Gibbons:1978ac
;
Mazur:1989by
of conformal rotation along the imaginary axis, i.e.,
h\to i\hat{h}
with a new real graviton trace
\hat{h}
. In addition, we should rescale the redefined
\hat{h}
so that the normalization state of its kinetic term matches that of
\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}
,
a_{\mu}
and
\Phi
. Altogether, we set

\displaystyle h=2i\hat{h},

(58)

and express the quadratic action of EMD theory as

\displaystyle\delta^{2}\mathcal{S}[\hat{h}_{\mu\nu},\hat{h},a_{\mu},\Phi]=\frac{1}{2}\int\mathrm{d}^{4}x\sqrt{\det\bar{g}}\,\phi_{m}\mathcal{H}^{m}_{n}\phi^{n},

(59)

where the kinetic operator
\mathcal{H}
operating on the effective fluctuations
\phi_{m}=\big\{\hat{h}_{\mu\nu},\hat{h},a_{\mu},\Phi\big\}
is obtained in the following Laplace-type form

\displaystyle\phi_{m}\mathcal{H}^{m}_{n}\phi^{n}=\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}\bigg\{I^{\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}\hat{h}_{\alpha\beta}}D_{\rho}D^{\rho}+R^{\mu\alpha\nu\beta}+R^{\mu\beta\nu\alpha}-2\left(\bar{F}^{\mu\alpha}\bar{F}^{\nu\beta}+\bar{F}^{\mu\beta}\bar{F}^{\nu\alpha}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\frac{1}{2}\left(\bar{g}^{\mu\alpha}R^{\nu\beta}+\bar{g}^{\nu\alpha}R^{\mu\beta}+\bar{g}^{\mu\beta}R^{\nu\alpha}+\bar{g}^{\nu\beta}R^{\mu\alpha}\right)+\Lambda\left(\bar{g}^{\mu\alpha}\bar{g}^{\nu\beta}+\bar{g}^{\mu\beta}\bar{g}^{\nu\alpha}\right)\bigg\}\hat{h}_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\hat{h}\big(D_{\rho}D^{\rho}+2\Lambda\big)\hat{h}+a_{\mu}\big(\bar{g}^{\mu\nu}D_{\rho}D^{\rho}-R^{\mu\nu}\big)a_{\nu}+\Phi D_{\rho}D^{\rho}\Phi-i\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}R^{\mu\nu}\hat{h}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-i\hat{h}R^{\mu\nu}\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}-\sqrt{2}\kappa\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}R^{\mu\nu}\Phi-\sqrt{2}\kappa\Phi R^{\mu\nu}\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}+2\kappa a_{\mu}\bar{F}^{\mu\rho}D_{\rho}\Phi-2\kappa\Phi\bar{F}^{\mu\rho}D_{\rho}a_{\mu}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}\bigg\{\left(D^{\mu}\bar{F}^{\alpha\nu}+D^{\nu}\bar{F}^{\alpha\mu}\right)+2\left(\bar{g}^{\mu\rho}\bar{F}^{\nu\alpha}+\bar{g}^{\nu\rho}\bar{F}^{\mu\alpha}-\bar{g}^{\mu\alpha}\bar{F}^{\nu\rho}-\bar{g}^{\nu\alpha}\bar{F}^{\mu\rho}\right)D_{\rho}\bigg\}a_{\alpha}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}a_{\alpha}\bigg\{\left(D^{\mu}\bar{F}^{\alpha\nu}+D^{\nu}\bar{F}^{\alpha\mu}\right)-2\left(\bar{g}^{\mu\rho}\bar{F}^{\nu\alpha}+\bar{g}^{\nu\rho}\bar{F}^{\mu\alpha}-\bar{g}^{\mu\alpha}\bar{F}^{\nu\rho}-\bar{g}^{\nu\alpha}\bar{F}^{\mu\rho}\right)D_{\rho}\bigg\}\hat{h}_{\mu\nu},

(60)

where each component is made symmetric or anti-symmetric with respect to the corresponding fluctuations and also associated with their Hermitian pair by adjusting up to a total derivative.
14
14

14

For example, one can use the following schematic treatment for all commuting fluctuations
\phi_{m}

\displaystyle\phi_{m}\mathbb{K}^{\rho}(D_{\rho}\phi_{n})=\frac{1}{2}\phi_{m}\Big(\mathbb{K}^{\rho}D_{\rho}-\frac{1}{2}(D_{\rho}\mathbb{K}^{\rho})\Big)\phi_{n}-\frac{1}{2}\phi_{n}\Big(\mathbb{K}^{\rho}D_{\rho}+\frac{1}{2}(D_{\rho}\mathbb{K}^{\rho})\Big)\phi_{m}.

(61)

The above operator form may look complicated but is now structured exactly as the schematic (
30
) from where one can explicitly read off the necessary matrices
I
,
P
and
N_{\rho}
. Components of the identity operator or effective metric
I
that controls the kinetic part of fluctuations and their projections are expressed as

\displaystyle\phi_{m}I^{mn}\phi_{n}

\displaystyle=\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}I^{\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}\hat{h}_{\alpha\beta}}\hat{h}_{\alpha\beta}+\hat{h}\hat{h}+a_{\mu}\bar{g}^{\mu\nu}a_{\nu}+\Phi\Phi.

(62)

Taking a trace over the above components will count how many off-shell degrees of freedom are effectively acting in the quadratic fluctuation loop of the theory. On the other hand, the matrices
P
and
N^{\rho}
that combinedly control all the minimal and non-minimal interactions between fluctuations in the kinetic operator (
3.2
) have the following components

\displaystyle\phi_{m}(N^{\rho})^{mn}\phi_{n}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{2}\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}\bigg(\bar{g}^{\mu\rho}\bar{F}^{\nu\alpha}+\bar{g}^{\nu\rho}\bar{F}^{\mu\alpha}-\bar{g}^{\mu\alpha}\bar{F}^{\nu\rho}-\bar{g}^{\nu\alpha}\bar{F}^{\mu\rho}\bigg)a_{\alpha}

\displaystyle\qquad-\sqrt{2}a_{\alpha}\bigg(\bar{g}^{\mu\rho}\bar{F}^{\nu\alpha}+\bar{g}^{\nu\rho}\bar{F}^{\mu\alpha}-\bar{g}^{\mu\alpha}\bar{F}^{\nu\rho}-\bar{g}^{\nu\alpha}\bar{F}^{\mu\rho}\bigg)\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle\qquad+a_{\mu}\Big(2\kappa\bar{F}^{\mu\rho}\Big)\Phi+\Phi\Big(-2\kappa\bar{F}^{\mu\rho}\Big)a_{\mu},

(63)

\displaystyle\phi_{m}P^{mn}\phi_{n}

\displaystyle=\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}\bigg(R^{\mu\alpha\nu\beta}+R^{\mu\beta\nu\alpha}-2\left(\bar{F}^{\mu\alpha}\bar{F}^{\nu\beta}+\bar{F}^{\mu\beta}\bar{F}^{\nu\alpha}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad-\frac{1}{2}\left(\bar{g}^{\mu\alpha}R^{\nu\beta}+\bar{g}^{\nu\alpha}R^{\mu\beta}+\bar{g}^{\mu\beta}R^{\nu\alpha}+\bar{g}^{\nu\beta}R^{\mu\alpha}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\Lambda\left(\bar{g}^{\mu\alpha}\bar{g}^{\nu\beta}+\bar{g}^{\mu\beta}\bar{g}^{\nu\alpha}\right)\bigg)\hat{h}_{\alpha\beta}+2\hat{h}\Lambda\hat{h}-a_{\mu}R^{\mu\nu}a_{\nu}

\displaystyle\quad-i\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}R^{\mu\nu}\hat{h}-i\hat{h}R^{\mu\nu}\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}+\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}\Big(-\sqrt{2}\kappa R^{\mu\nu}\Big)\Phi+\Phi\Big(-\sqrt{2}\kappa R^{\mu\nu}\Big)\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}\bigg(D^{\mu}\bar{F}^{\alpha\nu}+D^{\nu}\bar{F}^{\alpha\mu}\bigg)a_{\alpha}+\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}a_{\alpha}\bigg(D^{\mu}\bar{F}^{\alpha\nu}+D^{\nu}\bar{F}^{\alpha\mu}\bigg)\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}.

(64)

The above relations encode all information one needs to know about the quadratic fluctuation of EMD theory, which can be utilized further to write the operator form (
31
) in terms of the more generic matrices describing the gauge connection
\omega_{\rho}
, effective matrix-valued potential
E
and curvature commutator
\Omega_{\rho\sigma}
. In this progress, one first needs to derive the components of the contraction
\omega^{\rho}\omega_{\rho}
,

\displaystyle\phi_{m}(\omega^{\rho}\omega_{\rho})^{mn}\phi_{n}

\displaystyle=\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}\bigg(\bar{g}^{\mu\alpha}\bar{F}^{\rho\nu}\bar{F}^{{{\beta}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\rho}}}+\bar{g}^{\mu\beta}\bar{F}^{\rho\nu}\bar{F}^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\rho}}}+\bar{g}^{\nu\alpha}\bar{F}^{\rho\mu}\bar{F}^{{{\beta}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\rho}}}+\bar{g}^{\nu\beta}\bar{F}^{\rho\mu}\bar{F}^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\rho}}}

\displaystyle\quad-2\left(\bar{F}^{\mu\alpha}\bar{F}^{\nu\beta}+\bar{F}^{\mu\beta}\bar{F}^{\nu\alpha}\right)\bigg)\hat{h}_{\alpha\beta}+a_{\mu}\bigg(-\left(2+\kappa^{2}\right)\bar{F}^{\mu\rho}\bar{F}^{{{\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\rho}}}\bigg)a_{\nu}

\displaystyle\quad+\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}\bigg(-2\sqrt{2}\kappa\bar{F}^{\mu\rho}\bar{F}^{{{\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}\bigg)\Phi+\Phi\bigg(-2\sqrt{2}\kappa\bar{F}^{\mu\rho}\bar{F}^{{{\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}\bigg)\hat{h}_{\mu\nu},

(65)

followed by components of the commutation between two connections
\omega_{\rho}
and
\omega_{\sigma}
as

\displaystyle\phi_{m}[\omega_{\rho},\omega_{\sigma}]^{mn}\phi_{n}

\displaystyle=\phi_{m}(\omega_{\rho})^{mp}(\omega_{\sigma})^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{n}}}_{{{p}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\phi_{n}-\big(\rho\leftrightarrow\sigma\big),

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}\bigg(\left(\bar{g}^{\mu}_{\rho}\bar{F}^{\nu\theta}+\bar{g}^{\nu}_{\rho}\bar{F}^{\mu\theta}\right)\left(\bar{g}^{\alpha}_{\theta}\bar{F}^{{{\beta}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.79993pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.99994pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\sigma}}}+\bar{g}^{\beta}_{\theta}\bar{F}^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.79993pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.99994pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\sigma}}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad-\left(\bar{g}^{\mu}_{\rho}\bar{F}^{\nu\theta}+\bar{g}^{\nu}_{\rho}\bar{F}^{\mu\theta}\right)\left(\bar{g}^{\alpha}_{\sigma}\bar{F}^{{{\beta}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.809pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.809pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.30026pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64305pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\theta}}}+\bar{g}^{\beta}_{\sigma}\bar{F}^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.809pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.809pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.30026pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64305pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\theta}}}\right)

\displaystyle\text{Tr}(I)

Similarly, we will now evaluate the contributions from ghost fields that were excluded so far, including the trace data (
3.2
). Evidently, the operator describing the ghost action (
51
) is not in the prescribed Laplace-type form since the kinetic terms are not diagonalized. However, at any point, it is convenient to choose the following redefinitions

\displaystyle\begin{split}b_{\mu}\to\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}\left(c_{\mu}-ib_{\mu}\right),\kern 5.0ptb\to\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}\left(c-ib\right),\\

(71)

and express the kinetic operator acting on ghost fields
\phi_{m}=\big\{b_{\mu},c_{\mu},b,c\big\}
as

\displaystyle\phi_{m}\mathcal{H}^{m}_{n}\phi^{n}

\displaystyle=b_{\mu}\left(\bar{g}^{\mu\nu}\Box+R^{\mu\nu}\right)b_{\nu}+c_{\mu}\left(\bar{g}^{\mu\nu}\Box+R^{\mu\nu}\right)c_{\nu}+b\Box b+c\Box c

\displaystyle\quad+b_{\mu}\bar{F}^{\rho\mu}\left(D_{\rho}b+iD_{\rho}c\right)+c_{\mu}\bar{F}^{\rho\mu}\left(D_{\rho}c-iD_{\rho}b\right)

\displaystyle\quad-b\bar{F}^{\rho\mu}\left(D_{\rho}b_{\mu}-iD_{\rho}c_{\mu}\right)-c\bar{F}^{\rho\mu}\left(D_{\rho}c_{\mu}+iD_{\rho}b_{\mu}\right),

(72)

where
\Box=D_{\rho}D^{\rho}
. It is now straightforward to extract the matrices defined in (
30
) and (
31
),

\displaystyle\begin{split}\phi_{m}I^{mn}\phi_{n}&=b_{\mu}\bar{g}^{\mu\nu}b_{\nu}+c_{\mu}\bar{g}^{\mu\nu}c_{\nu}+bb+cc,\\
\phi_{m}P^{mn}\phi_{n}&=b_{\mu}R^{\mu\nu}c_{\nu}+c_{\mu}R^{\mu\nu}b_{\nu},\\
\phi_{m}(\omega^{\rho})^{mn}\phi_{n}&=\frac{1}{2}b_{\mu}\big(\bar{F}^{\rho\mu}\big)b-\frac{1}{2}b\big(\bar{F}^{\rho\mu}\big)b_{\mu}+\frac{1}{2}b_{\mu}\big(i\bar{F}^{\rho\mu}\big)c-\frac{1}{2}c\big(i\bar{F}^{\rho\mu}\big)b_{\mu}\\
&\quad+\frac{1}{2}c_{\mu}\big(\bar{F}^{\rho\mu}\big)c-\frac{1}{2}c\big(\bar{F}^{\rho\mu}\big)c_{\mu}+\frac{1}{2}c_{\mu}\big(i\bar{F}^{\rho\mu}\big)b-\frac{1}{2}b\big(i\bar{F}^{\rho\mu}\big)c_{\mu},\end{split}

(73)

followed by,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\phi_{m}E^{mn}\phi_{n}&=b_{\mu}R^{\mu\nu}c_{\nu}+c_{\mu}R^{\mu\nu}b_{\nu},\\
\phi_{m}\left(\Omega_{\rho\sigma}\right)^{mn}\phi_{n}&=b_{\mu}\Big(R^{{{\mu}{\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.79993pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.99994pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\rho}{\sigma}}}\Big)b_{\nu}+c_{\mu}\Big(R^{{{\mu}{\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.79993pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.99994pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\rho}{\sigma}}}\Big)c_{\nu}-\frac{1}{2}b_{\mu}\big(D^{\mu}\bar{F}_{\rho\sigma}\big)b\\
&\quad+\frac{1}{2}b\big(D^{\mu}\bar{F}_{\rho\sigma}\big)b_{\mu}-\frac{1}{2}b_{\mu}\big(iD^{\mu}\bar{F}_{\rho\sigma}\big)c+\frac{1}{2}c\big(iD^{\mu}\bar{F}_{\rho\sigma}\big)b_{\mu}\\
&\quad+\frac{1}{2}c_{\mu}\big(iD^{\mu}\bar{F}_{\rho\sigma}\big)b-\frac{1}{2}b\big(iD^{\mu}\bar{F}_{\rho\sigma}\big)c_{\mu}-\frac{1}{2}c_{\mu}\big(D^{\mu}\bar{F}_{\rho\sigma}\big)c\\
&\quad+\frac{1}{2}c\big(D^{\mu}\bar{F}_{\rho\sigma}\big)c_{\mu},\end{split}

(74)

where all other valid components are completely dissolved due to our specific background, equations of motion and Maxwell-Bianchi identities (
3.1
). The calculated traces of
I
,
E
,
E^{2}
and
\Omega_{\rho\sigma}\Omega^{\rho\sigma}
for ghost fields are

\displaystyle\begin{split}\text{Tr}(I)&=4+4+1+1=10,\\
\text{Tr}(E)&=8\Lambda,\kern 5.0pt\text{Tr}(E^{2})=2R_{\mu\nu}R^{\mu\nu},\\
\text{Tr}\left(\Omega_{\rho\sigma}\Omega^{\rho\sigma}\right)&=-2R_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}R^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}.\end{split}

(75)

Finally, the Seeley-DeWitt coefficients for the
U(1)
-charged EMD-AdS theory can be achieved by appropriately utilizing both the gauge-fixed (
3.2
) and ghost (
75
) trace data into the formulae (
32
) to (
34
). The ghost fields in (
51
) are bosons, hence their contribution must be associated with an overall minus signature, i.e., by setting
\chi=-1
because of their reverse spin-statistics. Up to the third order, the Seeley-DeWitt coefficient results are

\displaystyle\begin{split}(4\pi)^{2}{a_{0}}^{\text{$U(1)$-EMD}}(x)&=5,\\[5.0pt]
(4\pi)^{2}{a_{2}}^{\text{$U(1)$-EMD}}(x)&=-\frac{38}{3}\Lambda,\\[5.0pt]
(4\pi)^{2}{a_{4}}^{\text{$U(1)$-EMD}}(x)&=\frac{10}{9}R_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}R^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}+\left(\frac{\kappa^{4}}{6}-\kappa^{2}+\frac{5}{36}\right)R_{\mu\nu}R^{\mu\nu}\\
&\qquad-\left(\frac{\kappa^{4}}{24}-\frac{\kappa^{2}}{4}+\frac{55}{72}\right)\Lambda^{2}.\end{split}

(76)

The same results for the
U(1)
-charged EMD theory in flat space are achieved by simply setting
\Lambda=0
into the above formulas, or one can proceed without the cosmological constant from the beginning. The
{a_{4}}^{\text{$U(1)$-EMD}}
relation is going to be one of the central results of this paper. Before proceeding further into its implication in logarithmic correction to black hole entropy, we find it worth making a few remarks:

1.

Even though the trace data (
3.2
) includes the
R_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\bar{F}^{\mu\nu}\bar{F}^{\rho\sigma}
terms,
{a_{4}}^{\text{$U(1)$-EMD}}
is found to be free from any such term. They are exactly canceled out inside the final formula due to our specific choice of embedding EM backgrounds into the EMD system. This fact suggests that
{a_{4}}(x)
for EMD fluctuations is always invariant under the electro-magnetic duality transformation.

2.

The
{a_{4}}^{\text{$U(1)$-EMD}}
form in
(
76
)
is expressed only in terms of the background metric invariants. However, it is never advisable to ignore the terms involving background strength
\bar{F}_{\mu\nu}
from the beginning or in any intermediate steps of trace calculations. This is because some part of the coefficient associated with
R_{\mu\nu}R^{\mu\nu}
and
\Lambda^{2}
(or
R^{2}
) contributions are induced from the
\bar{F}_{\mu\rho}\bar{F}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\rho}}}_{{{\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}
terms through the Einstein equation (
3.1
). In this context, one should note that the
R_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}R^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}
contribution is fundamental for a theory with fixed degrees of freedom. Thus, the coefficient of
R_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}R^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}
contribution is fully insensitive to the non-minimal couplings and gauge interactions between fields.

3.

It is not trivial to recover the results of an Einstein-Maxwell theory
Bhattacharyya:2012ss
from (
76
) even after setting the dilaton coupling constant
\kappa=0
. This is because the
\kappa
-independent terms of Seeley-DeWitt data (
76
) are encoded with all off-shell degrees of freedom of the EMD fluctuations including the dilaton.

4.

The
{a_{4}}^{\text{$U(1)$-EMD}}
value exhibits an exact match with the result of
Castro:2018tg
(see eq. (A.18)) for a pure Kaluza-Klein system with the special limiting case
\kappa=\sqrt{3}
and
\Lambda=0
. This essentially boosts our confidence regarding the consistency of the Seeley-DeWitt coefficient results (as well as the relevant logarithmic corrections in
sections

4
and
5
) reported in this paper for more generic EMD and EMD-AdS models with arbitrary dilaton coupling
\kappa
.

4
Logarithmic correction for black holes in EMD and EMD-AdS theories

In this section, we shall calculate and demonstrate the relevant treatment for the logarithmic correction to the entropy of non-extremal and extremal black holes embedded in the four-dimensional
U(1)
-charged EMD-AdS and EMD theories.

4.1
Trace anomalies and central charges

Logarithmic corrections are known to be directly connected to the divergent part of trace anomalies for a gravity theory with quantum corrections.
16
16

16

Readers may be interested in a relevant discussion in section 7 of
Keeler:2014nn
.
In their present analysis via the heat kernel treatment, the four-dimensional anomaly data are encoded inside the third-order Seeley-DeWitt coefficient
a_{4}(x)
. For the 4D EMD theories embedded with the Einstein-Maxwell (EM) backgrounds in AdS space (or with a negative cosmological constant
\Lambda
), we will always end up expressing,

\displaystyle a_{4}(x)=\frac{1}{16\pi^{2}}\Big(c_{\text{A}}W_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}W^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}-a_{\text{A}}E_{4}+b_{\text{A}}R^{2}\Big),

(77)

where the coefficients (
c_{\text{A}},a_{\text{A}}
) are the central charges of the theory related to the Type-A trace anomalies – Weyl anomaly, i.e., the Weyl tensor squared term
W_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}W^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}
and four-dimensional Euler density
E_{4}
. In terms of the background metric, one can explicitly define,

\displaystyle\begin{split}W_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}W^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}&=R_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}R^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}-2R_{\mu\nu}R^{\mu\nu}+\frac{1}{3}R^{2},\\
E_{4}&=R_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}R^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}-4R_{\mu\nu}R^{\mu\nu}+R^{2},\end{split}

(78)

where the classical Einstein equation implies
R=4\Lambda=-\dfrac{12}{\ell^{2}}
, with
\ell
being the radius of EM-AdS background solutions. Interestingly, the Seeley-DeWitt expansion method used in this paper can compute the
c_{\text{A}}
,
a_{\text{A}}
and
b_{\text{A}}
coefficients related to four-derivative invariants by analyzing the quadratic fluctuation data of a two-derivative action. Here one should note that in the flat case limit (i.e.,
\Lambda=0
or
\ell\to\infty
) of EM-AdS backgrounds, the Ricci scalar
R
vanishes, and consequently, the anomaly relation (
77
) is entirely controlled by the central charges
c_{\text{A}}
and
a_{\text{A}}
. For the four-dimensional EMD-AdS theory, the
a_{4}(x)
result (
76
) extracts the following trace anomaly data

\displaystyle\begin{split}c_{\text{A}}&=\frac{1}{24}\left(2\kappa^{4}-12\kappa^{2}+55\right),\\[3.0pt]
a_{\text{A}}&=\frac{1}{72}\left(6\kappa^{4}-36\kappa^{2}+85\right),\\[3.0pt]
b_{\text{A}}&=\frac{1}{72}\left(\kappa^{4}-6\kappa^{2}-25\right).\end{split}

(79)

In the flat limit of EMD-AdS theory, we will always find
b_{\text{A}}=0
while the central charge data (
c_{\text{A}},a_{\text{A}}
) are the same as above. Next, we aim to utilize the trace anomaly results (
79
) in the formula (
28b
) or alternatively use the following relation

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}=\frac{1}{16\pi^{2}}\int_{\text{BH}}\mathrm{d}^{4}x\sqrt{\det\bar{g}}\left(c_{\text{A}}W_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}W^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}-a_{\text{A}}E_{4}+b_{\text{A}}R^{2}\right),

(80)

and then structure the explicit formulas capturing
\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}
contributions to calculate the logarithmic corrections for Kerr-AdS, Reissner-Nordström-AdS, and Schwarzschild-AdS as well as Kerr, Reissner-Nordström, and Schwarzschild black hole embedded in EMD-AdS and EMD theories, respectively. However, this progress demands a pressing need for the integrated
W_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}W^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}
,
E_{4}
and
R^{2}
invariants over the appropriate part of horizon or near-horizon geometries in non-extremal and extremal limits of the black hole backgrounds.

4.2
Non-extremal black hole backgrounds and
\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}
formulas

We will start with a generic background of rotating and charged asymptotically-AdS black hole in four-dimensional spacetimes. The relevant metric is given in terms of appropriate Boyer-Lindquist coordinates as

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}s^{2}=\bar{g}_{\mu\nu}\mathrm{d}x^{\mu}\mathrm{d}x^{\nu}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\Delta_{r}}{\rho^{2}}\left(\mathrm{d}t-\frac{a\sin^{2}\theta}{\Xi}\mathrm{d}\phi\right)^{2}+\frac{\rho^{2}}{\Delta_{r}}\mathrm{d}r^{2}+\frac{\rho^{2}}{\Delta_{\theta}}\mathrm{d}\theta^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{\Delta_{\theta}\sin^{2}\theta}{\rho^{2}}\left(a\,\mathrm{d}t-\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{\Xi}\mathrm{d}\phi\right)^{2},

(81)

where we have followed the convention of
Caldarelli:1999x
and set
G_{D}=1
. The parameters
\Delta_{r}
,
\Delta_{\theta}
,
\rho
and
\Xi
are defined as

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}\Delta_{r}=(r^{2}+a^{2})\bigg(1+\frac{r^{2}}{\ell^{2}}\bigg)-2mr+q^{2}+p^{2},\\
\Delta_{\theta}=1-\frac{a^{2}}{\ell^{2}}\cos^{2}\theta,\quad\rho^{2}=r^{2}+a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta,\quad\Xi=1-\frac{a^{2}}{\ell^{2}}.\end{gathered}

The physical mass
M
, angular momentum
J
, electric charge
Q
and magnetic charge
P
of the black hole are characterized by the parameters
m
,
a
,
q
and
p
via the relations,

\displaystyle M=\frac{m}{\Xi^{2}},\quad J=\frac{ma}{\Xi^{2}},\quad Q=\frac{q}{\Xi},\quad P=\frac{p}{\Xi}.

(84)

Here the rotational parameter
a
must satisfy the
a<\ell
for
a\geq 0
limit so that the metric (
4.2
) represents an AdS black hole. The background metric (
4.2
) also solves the Maxwell part of the field equations (
3.1
) for the gauge field,

\displaystyle\bar{A}=-\frac{qr}{\rho^{2}}\left(\mathrm{d}t-\frac{a\sin^{2}\theta}{\Xi}\mathrm{d}\phi\right)-\frac{p\cos\theta}{\rho^{2}}\left(a\,\mathrm{d}t-\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{\Xi}\mathrm{d}\phi\right),

(85)

followed by its strength
\bar{F}_{\mu\nu}
expressing,

\displaystyle\bar{F}_{\mu\nu}\bar{F}^{\mu\nu}=-\frac{2}{\left(r^{2}+a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta\right)^{4}}

\displaystyle\bigg[\left(q^{2}-p^{2}\right)\left(r^{4}-6a^{2}r^{2}\cos^{2}\theta+a^{4}\cos^{4}\theta\right)

\displaystyle\kern 5.0pt+8qpar\cos\theta\left(r^{2}-a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta\right)\bigg].

(86)

In this paper, our specific choice backgrounds are those with
q=p
,
a=0
and
q,p=0
so that
\bar{F}_{\mu\nu}\bar{F}^{\mu\nu}
invariant vanishes, and we can embed them into EMD theories (as discussed in
section

3.1
). Now, if
r=r_{+}
is the position of the event horizon for solving
\Delta_{r}=0
as the largest real root, such that

\displaystyle m=\frac{1}{2{r_{+}}\ell^{2}}\Big[{r_{+}}^{4}+\left(\ell^{2}+a^{2}\right){r_{+}}^{2}+\left(a^{2}+q_{e}^{2}\right)\ell^{2}\Big],

(87)

then the Bekenstein-Hawking entropy and inverse Hawking temperature are given by

\displaystyle S_{\text{BH}}=\frac{4\pi\left(r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}\right)}{\Xi},\quad\beta=\frac{4\pi\left(r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}\right)}{r_{+}\left(1+\frac{a^{2}}{\ell^{2}}+3\frac{r_{+}^{2}}{\ell^{2}}-\frac{a^{2}+q_{e}^{2}}{r_{+}^{2}}\right)},

(88)

where
q_{e}=\sqrt{q^{2}+p^{2}}
. With the above background setup, we express the curvature invariants required in (
77
) as

\displaystyle R^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{144}{\ell^{4}},

\displaystyle W_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}W^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}

\displaystyle=\frac{48}{\left(r^{2}+a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta\right)^{6}}\bigg[8r^{4}\left(q_{e}^{2}-2mr\right)^{2}-m^{2}\left(r^{2}+a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta\right)^{3}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-8r^{2}\left(q_{e}^{2}-3mr\right)\left(q_{e}^{2}-2mr\right)\left(r^{2}+a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\Big(q_{e}^{4}-10mrq_{e}^{2}+18m^{2}r^{2}\Big)\left(r^{2}+a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta\right)^{2}\bigg],

\displaystyle E_{4}

\displaystyle=\frac{24}{\ell^{4}}+\frac{8}{\left(r^{2}+a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta\right)^{6}}\bigg[48r^{4}\left(q_{e}^{2}-2mr\right)^{2}-6m^{2}\left(r^{2}+a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta\right)^{3}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-48r^{2}\left(q_{e}^{2}-3mr\right)\left(q_{e}^{2}-2mr\right)\left(r^{2}+a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\Big(5q_{e}^{4}-60mrq_{e}^{2}+108m^{2}r^{2}\Big)\left(r^{2}+a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta\right)^{2}\bigg].

(89)

Next, the above invariants need to be integrated over the AdS
4
black hole geometry (
4.2
). But, upon integration, these
a_{4}(x)
invariants will diverge due to the infinite volume of AdS
4
. In this paper, we aim to tame these divergences by following the prescription of holographic renormalization
Skenderis:2002wp
. The underlying treatment is to set a large cut-off
r=r_{c}
at the boundary of the geometry (
4.2
) and followed by adding the holographic counterterm,

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{\text{HCT}}=\int_{\partial\mathcal{M}}\mathrm{d}^{3}y\sqrt{\det\gamma}\left(c_{1}+c_{2}\mathcal{R}\right),

(90)

where
\mathcal{R}
is the Ricci scalar related to the metric
\gamma
describing the boundary geometry
\partial\mathcal{M}
. Then, we determine the appropriate values for
c_{1}
and
c_{2}
coefficients such that the combined bulk-boundary contribution
\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}+\mathcal{C}_{\text{HCT}}
receives a finite value for (
80
) in the limit
r_{c}\to\infty
. In this process, we obtain the following renormalized results for the integrated
W_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}W^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}
,
E_{4}
and
R^{2}
invariants,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\frac{1}{16\pi^{2}}\int\mathrm{d}^{4}x\sqrt{\det\bar{g}}\,E_{4}&=4,\\
\frac{1}{16\pi^{2}}\int\mathrm{d}^{4}x\sqrt{\det\bar{g}}\,R^{2}&=R_{1}+\beta R_{2},\\
\frac{1}{16\pi^{2}}\int\mathrm{d}^{4}x\sqrt{\det\bar{g}}\,W_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}W^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}&=\frac{W_{1}}{\beta}+W_{2}+\beta W_{3},\end{split}

(91a)

where the
\beta
-independent parts of background Ricci and Weyl squared integrations are expressed as

\displaystyle R_{1}

\displaystyle=-\frac{24}{\Xi\ell^{2}}\left(r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}\right),\quad R_{2}=\frac{12}{\pi\Xi\ell^{4}}\left(\ell^{2}+r_{+}^{2}\right)r_{+},

\displaystyle W_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{\pi\left(a^{2}+r_{+}^{2}\right)}{\Xi a^{5}r_{+}^{2}}\bigg[\left(3a^{4}+2a^{2}r_{+}^{2}+3r_{+}^{4}\right)ar_{+}-3\left(r_{+}^{4}-a^{4}\right)\left(a^{2}+r_{+}^{2}\right)\arctan\left(\frac{a}{r}_{+}\right)\bigg],

\displaystyle W_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\left(a^{2}+r_{+}^{2}\right)}{2\Xi a^{5}\ell^{2}r_{+}^{3}}\bigg[\Big(3a^{4}\left(\ell^{2}-r_{+}^{2}\right)+4a^{2}\ell^{2}r_{+}^{2}-3\left(\ell^{2}+3r_{+}^{2}\right)r_{+}^{4}\Big)ar_{+}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad\quad\kern 5.0pt-3\left(r_{+}^{4}-a^{4}\right)\Big(a^{2}\left(\ell^{2}-r_{+}^{2}\right)-\left(\ell^{2}+3r_{+}^{2}\right)r_{+}^{2}\Big)\arctan\left(\frac{a}{r}_{+}\right)\bigg],

\displaystyle W_{3}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{16\pi\Xi a^{5}\ell^{4}\left(r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}\right)r_{+}^{4}}\bigg[3a^{9}\left(\ell^{2}-r_{+}^{2}\right)^{2}r_{+}-4a^{7}\left(3r_{+}^{4}+12\ell^{2}r_{+}^{2}+\ell^{4}\right)r_{+}^{3}

\displaystyle\qquad+2a^{5}\left(5r_{+}^{4}-14\ell^{2}r_{+}^{2}+\ell^{4}\right)r_{+}^{5}-4a^{3}\left(\ell^{4}-9r_{+}^{4}\right)r_{+}^{7}+3a\left(\ell^{2}+3r_{+}^{2}\right)^{2}r_{+}^{9}

\displaystyle\qquad-3\left(r_{+}^{4}-a^{4}\right)\left(a^{2}+r_{+}^{2}\right)\Big(a^{2}\left(\ell^{2}-r_{+}^{2}\right)-\left(\ell^{2}+3r_{+}^{2}\right)r_{+}^{2}\Big)^{2}\arctan\left(\frac{a}{r}_{+}\right)\bigg],

(91b)

where
\Xi=1-a^{2}/\ell^{2}
. Note that the above derivations considered the analytical continuation
t\to-i\tau
of the metric (
4.2
), where the Euclideanized time
\tau
is identified as a periodic coordinate of period
\beta
. Also, the integrations are executed in the range
r_{+}\leq r\leq r_{c}
(for the bulk part),
0\leq\theta\leq\pi
and
0\leq\phi\leq 2\pi
. For more details about the holographic renormalization and boundary counterterm, we refer to
Appendix

B
. The choice of holographic renormalization is natural since the logarithmic correction is an explicit correction term to the on-shell or bulk effective action. Hence, the current prescription is very convenient and always found to be providing a physically sensible, unambiguous and finite result for all the AdS
4
black holes. In this line, a strong validation can be found in
David:2021eoq
where it has been proved that the holographic counterterm (
90
) exactly matches the standard boundary term of the Gauss-Bonnet-Chern theorem
Chern:1945wp
.
17
17

17

Please review Appendix E of
David:2021eoq
.
Another solid verification emerges when the holographic renormalization procedure computes the correct and exact Euler characteristic value via integrating the Euler density
E_{4}
around 4D black hole geometries as

\displaystyle\chi=\lim_{r_{c}\to\infty}\left[\frac{1}{32\pi^{2}}\int_{\mathcal{M}}\mathrm{d}^{4}x\sqrt{\det\bar{g}}\,E_{4}+\int_{\partial\mathcal{M}}\mathrm{d}^{3}y\sqrt{\det\gamma}\left(c_{1}+c_{2}\mathcal{R}\right)\right]=2.

(92)

Finally, substitution of the relations (
91
) into (
80
) with appropriate limits provides the
\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}
formulas of the non-extremal black holes embedded in EMD-AdS theory. For the Schwarzschild-AdS (
q,p,a=0
), Reissner-Nordström-AdS (
q=p,a=0
) and Kerr-AdS (
q,p=0
), we successively obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}^{\text{(Sch-AdS)}}&=-4a_{\text{A}}+\frac{4}{\left(3r_{+}^{2}+\ell^{2}\right)\ell^{2}}\bigg[\left(c_{\text{A}}-6b_{\text{A}}\right)r_{+}^{4}+\left(2c_{\text{A}}+6b_{\text{A}}\right)\ell^{2}r_{+}^{2}+c_{\text{A}}\ell^{4}\bigg],\\[5.0pt]
\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}^{\text{(RN-AdS)}}&=\frac{4}{5}\left(c_{\text{A}}-5a_{\text{A}}\right)-\frac{4}{5\pi\ell^{4}r_{+}}\bigg[\left(7c_{\text{A}}+30b_{\text{A}}\right)\pi\ell^{2}r_{+}^{3}-\frac{8\pi^{2}c_{\text{A}}\ell^{4}r_{+}^{2}}{\beta}\\
&\qquad\quad\qquad-\frac{\beta}{2}\Big(\left(c_{\text{A}}+30b_{\text{A}}\right)\ell^{2}r_{+}^{2}+\left(4c_{\text{A}}+30b_{\text{A}}\right)r_{+}^{4}+c_{\text{A}}\ell^{4}\Big)\bigg],\\[5.0pt]
\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}^{\text{(Kerr-AdS)}}&=-4a_{\text{A}}+\frac{\beta}{\pi\ell^{2}\left(r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}\right)\left(\ell^{2}-a^{2}\right)r_{+}}\bigg[6b_{\text{A}}a^{4}\ell^{2}-c_{\text{A}}a^{2}\ell^{4}\\
&\qquad\quad\qquad+\left(c_{\text{A}}-6b_{\text{A}}\right)r_{+}^{6}+\Big(\left(2c_{\text{A}}+6b_{\text{A}}\right)\ell^{2}-\left(c_{\text{A}}+12b_{\text{A}}\right)a^{2}\Big)r_{+}^{4}\\
&\qquad\quad\qquad+\Big(c_{\text{A}}\ell^{4}+\left(12b_{\text{A}}-2c_{\text{A}}\right)a^{2}\ell^{2}-6b_{\text{A}}a^{4}\Big)r_{+}^{2}\bigg].\end{split}

(93)

In the flat space limit
\ell\to\infty
, the integrations (
91
) exactly reproduce the known relations in
Sen:2013ns
;
Charles:2015nn
;
Karan:2020sk
. The corresponding
\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}
formulas for the asymptotically-flat and non-extremal black holes embedded in EMD theory are

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}^{\text{(Sch)}}&=4(c_{\text{A}}-a_{\text{A}}),\quad\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}^{\text{(Kerr)}}=4(c_{\text{A}}-a_{\text{A}}),\\[3.0pt]
\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}^{\text{(RN)}}&=4(c_{\text{A}}-a_{\text{A}})+\frac{2c_{\text{A}}\beta q_{e}^{4}}{5\pi r_{+}^{5}},\end{split}

(94)

where
\beta=4\pi r_{+}^{3}/\left(r_{+}^{2}-q_{e}^{2}\right)
,
r_{+}=m+\sqrt{m^{2}-q_{e}^{2}}
and
q_{e}=\sqrt{2}q=\sqrt{2}p
.

4.3
Extremal limit, near-horizon backgrounds and
\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}
formulas

The special treatments required to proceed with the extremal black holes are already summarized in
section

2.2
. Here the most trivial approach is to take the extremal or zero-temperature limit
\beta\to\infty
of the
\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}
contribution (
80
) through the integrated invariant relations (
91
). However, the direct use of extremal limit naively leads to divergences. But, we employ a systematic approach (following the work
David:2021eoq
) to escape from the extremal divergence and extract a finite piece of
\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}
contribution. By keeping the black hole parameters
a,\,\ell,\,q,\,p
fixed, a
low-temperature expansion
of horizon radius
r_{+}
in the limit
\beta\to\infty
can be managed as

\displaystyle r_{+}=r_{0}+\frac{2\pi\ell_{2}^{2}}{\beta}+\mathcal{O}(\beta^{-2}),

(95)

where the parameter
r_{0}
characterizing the ‘finite part’ is the extremal horizon radius and
\ell_{2}
is recognized as an AdS
2
radius. The appearance of an AdS
2
part inside the structure of extremal black holes is quite natural. For example, see the prescription of quantum entropy function formalism
Sen:2008wa
;
Sen:2009wb
;
Sen:2009wc
. The extremal parameters (
r_{0}
,
\ell_{2}
) are related via the AdS
4
radius and charges in the extremal limit as

\displaystyle\begin{split}\ell^{2}_{2}&=\frac{\ell^{2}\left(a^{2}+r_{0}^{2}\right)}{\left(\ell^{2}+a^{2}+6r_{0}^{2}\right)},\\[3.0pt]
a^{2}+q_{e}^{2}&=\frac{r_{0}^{2}\left(a^{2}+\ell^{2}+3r_{0}^{2}\right)}{\ell^{2}},\end{split}

(96)

where we must consider
r_{0}^{2}>(\ell_{2}^{2}-a^{2})
. Now, using the typical form (
95
) of full horizon radius
r_{+}
, we can systematically adjust the
\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}
contribution up to a finite constant as

\displaystyle\lim_{\beta\to\infty}\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}\equiv\mathcal{C}_{0}+\mathcal{C}_{1}\beta+\mathcal{O}(\beta^{-1}).

(97)

The part linear in
\beta
is divergent and can be neglected since it is nothing but an infinite shift in the ground state energy of one-loop effective action. Therefore, after setting the extremal limit
\beta\to\infty
, all the terms inverse in
\beta
vanished and we can recognize the finite piece
\mathcal{C}_{0}
as an unambiguous and effective contribution to
\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}
. With this setup, the related extremal version of the curvature invariant integrations (
91
) are obtained as

\displaystyle\lim_{\beta\to\infty}\frac{1}{16\pi^{2}}\int\mathrm{d}^{4}x\sqrt{\det\bar{g}}\,E_{4}=4,

\displaystyle\lim_{\beta\to\infty}\frac{1}{16\pi^{2}}\int\mathrm{d}^{4}x\sqrt{\det\bar{g}}\,R^{2}=-\frac{24\left(a^{4}+4a^{2}r_{0}^{2}+3r_{0}^{4}\right)}{\left(\ell^{2}-a^{2}\right)\left(a^{2}+6r_{0}^{2}+\ell^{2}\right)},

\displaystyle\lim_{\beta\to\infty}\frac{1}{16\pi^{2}}\int\mathrm{d}^{4}x\sqrt{\det\bar{g}}\,W_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}W^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}=\frac{1}{8a^{4}r_{0}^{5}\left(\ell^{2}-a^{2}\right)\left(a^{2}+r_{0}^{2}\right)\left(a^{2}+6r_{0}^{2}+\ell^{2}\right)}\bigg[

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+a^{2}r_{0}\Big(9a^{8}\left(2r_{0}^{2}\ell^{2}-r_{0}^{4}-\ell^{4}\right)+a^{6}r_{0}^{2}\left(70r_{0}^{2}\ell^{2}-135r_{0}^{4}+\ell^{4}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+2a^{4}r_{0}^{4}\left(106r_{0}^{2}\ell^{2}-77r_{0}^{4}+27\ell^{4}\right)+6a^{2}r_{0}^{6}\left(18r_{0}^{2}\ell^{2}-13r_{0}^{4}+3\ell^{4}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-3r_{0}^{8}\left(2r_{0}^{2}\ell^{2}+15r_{0}^{4}-\ell^{4}\right)\Big)-3r_{0}^{11}\left(3r_{0}^{2}+\ell^{2}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad-12a^{3}\left(a^{2}+r_{0}^{2}\right){}^{2}\Big(a^{2}\left(r_{0}^{2}-\ell^{2}\right)+r_{0}^{2}\left(3r_{0}^{2}+\ell^{2}\right)\Big){}^{2}\arctan\left(\frac{a}{r_{0}}\right)\bigg].

(98)

We will utilize the above relations and express the
\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}
contribution (
80
) generally in terms of the five independent parameters
\left\{r_{0},\ell,a,q,p\right\}
. For a Schwarzschild background (
q,p,a=0
), extremality is not a valid limit due to having a vanishing extremal horizon
r_{0}
. However, for the extremal Reissner-Nordström-AdS (
q=p,a=0
) and Kerr-AdS (
q,p=0
) embedded in EMD-AdS theory, we derive the following explicit
\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}
formulas

\displaystyle\lim_{\beta\to\infty}\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}^{\text{(RN-AdS)}}

\displaystyle=\frac{4}{3}\left(c_{\text{A}}-3a_{\text{A}}+3b_{\text{A}}\right)-\frac{2}{3r_{0}^{2}\ell_{2}^{2}}\left(c_{\text{A}}+3b_{\text{A}}\right)\left(\ell_{2}^{4}+r_{0}^{4}\right),

\displaystyle\lim_{\beta\to\infty}\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}^{\text{(Kerr-AdS)}}

\displaystyle=-4a_{\text{A}}+\frac{2}{r_{0}^{2}\left(\ell^{2}-a^{2}\right)\left(a^{2}+r_{0}^{2}\right)\left(a^{2}+6r_{0}^{2}+\ell^{2}\right)}\bigg[c_{\text{A}}a^{4}\ell^{4}

\displaystyle\quad+3\left(c_{\text{A}}-6b_{\text{A}}\right)r_{0}^{8}+\Big(\left(4c_{\text{A}}-42b_{\text{A}}\right)a^{2}+\left(2c_{\text{A}}+6b_{\text{A}}\right)\ell^{2}\Big)r_{0}^{6}

\displaystyle\quad+\Big(\left(8c_{\text{A}}+12b_{\text{A}}\right)a^{2}\ell^{2}-\left(3c_{\text{A}}+30b_{\text{A}}\right)a^{4}-c_{\text{A}}\ell^{4}\Big)r_{0}^{4}

\displaystyle\quad+\Big(4c_{\text{A}}a^{2}\ell^{4}+\left(6b_{\text{A}}-2c_{\text{A}}\right)a^{4}\ell^{2}-6b_{\text{A}}a^{6}\Big)r_{0}^{2}\bigg].

(99)

In flat space (
\ell\to\infty
), the extremal limit is acquired by setting the constraints,

\displaystyle\ell_{2}=\sqrt{r_{0}^{2}+a^{2}},\quad r_{0}=\sqrt{a^{2}+q_{e}^{2}},

(100)

where
r_{0}
is identified as the mass parameter
m
of asymptotically-flat black holes (see, e.g.
Bhattacharyya:2012ss
;
Karan:2020sk
). Thus, the related
\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}
formulas for extremal black holes in EMD theory are

\displaystyle\begin{split}\lim_{\beta\to\infty}\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}^{\text{(RN)}}&=-4a_{\text{A}},\quad\lim_{\beta\to\infty}\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}^{\text{(Kerr)}}=4(c_{\text{A}}-a_{\text{A}}).\end{split}

(101)

Next, we will verify the consistency of the above formulas using the prescription of quantum entropy function (QEF) formalism
Sen:2008wa
;
Sen:2009wb
;
Sen:2009wc
. QEF is the most convenient and successful approach for the quantum entropy of extremal black holes where the underlying setup entirely relies on the near-horizon geometry analysis (see the discussion in
section

2.2
). In order to achieve the extremal near-horizon (ENH) geometry of the black hole (
4.2
), we cast the following coordinate transformations in terms of a new parameter
\lambda
(see, e.g.
David:2021eoq
;
Bhattacharyya:2012ss
)

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}r=r_{0}+\lambda\tilde{r},\quad t=\frac{\ell_{2}^{2}}{\lambda}\tilde{t},\quad\phi=\tilde{\phi}+\frac{a\left(\ell^{2}-a^{2}\right)}{\ell^{2}\left(a^{2}+r_{0}^{2}\right)}t,\\
\frac{r_{0}^{2}}{\ell^{2}}\left(3r_{0}^{2}+a^{2}+\ell^{2}\right)=a^{2}+q_{e}^{2}+\lambda^{2},\end{gathered}

and then set the limit
\lambda\to 0
by keeping
\tilde{r}
and
\tilde{t}
fixed. Further considering the analytical continuation
\tilde{t}\to-i\tau
, the metric describing Euclideanized ENH geometry is expressed as

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}s^{2}

\displaystyle=(\bar{g}_{\mu\nu}\mathrm{d}x^{\mu}\mathrm{d}x^{\nu})_{\text{ENH}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\ell_{2}^{2}\left(r_{0}^{2}+a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta\right)}{a^{2}+r_{0}^{2}}\left(\tilde{r}^{2}\mathrm{d}\tau^{2}+\frac{\mathrm{d}\tilde{r}^{2}}{\tilde{r}^{2}}\right)+\frac{\ell^{2}\left(r_{0}^{2}+a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta\right)}{\ell^{2}-a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta}\mathrm{d}\theta^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{\ell^{2}\left(a^{2}+r_{0}^{2}\right)^{2}\left(\ell^{2}-a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta\right)\sin^{2}\theta}{\left(\ell^{2}-a^{2}\right)^{2}\left(r_{0}^{2}+a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta\right)}\left(\mathrm{d}\tilde{\phi}-\frac{2\ell_{2}^{2}ar_{0}\left(\ell^{2}-a^{2}\right)}{\ell^{2}\left(a^{2}+r_{0}^{2}\right)^{2}}ir\mathrm{d}\tau\right)^{2}.

(104)

The above geometry form evidently accommodates an AdS
2
piece with coordinates
(\tau,\tilde{r})
, as required in the QEF prescription. Hence, before we proceed further and calculate
\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}
and the related integrated invariants, we must remove the divergence arising due to the infinite volume of AdS
2
geometry. By a suitable regularization, one can set an infrared cut-off at
\tilde{r}=\tilde{r}_{c}
and evaluate a regularized
\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}
in the range,

\displaystyle 1\leq\tilde{r}\leq\tilde{r}_{c},\quad 0\leq\tau\leq 2\pi,\quad 0\leq\theta\leq\pi,\quad 0\leq\tilde{\phi}\leq 2\pi.

(105)

One may naively think that the present treatment leads to ambiguous
\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}
results that vary in different regularization schemes. But, we want to ensure that the QEF formalism precisely identified that only the cut-off independent finite piece of ENH geometry induces the quantum horizon degeneracy and related corrections to the extremal black hole entropies
Sen:2008wa
;
Sen:2009wb
;
Sen:2009wc
. Thus, in terms of the ENH background (
4.3
), the finite
\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}
contribution for extremal AdS
4
black holes is now defined as

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}=-2\pi\int_{\text{ENH}}\mathrm{d}\theta\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{\phi}\,\mathcal{G}(\theta)a_{4}(x),

(106)

where
-2\pi
factor is arising from the
\tilde{r}_{c}
independent part of regularized AdS
2
volume
18
18

18

The regularized AdS
2
volume is
\sim\int_{0}^{\tilde{r}_{c}}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\tilde{r}\mathrm{d}\tau=2\pi\left(\tilde{r}_{c}-1\right)
. In any arbitrary regularization scheme, the cut-off dependent part
2\pi\tilde{r}_{c}
will be absorbed by either redefining the ground state energy in dual CFT
1
or adding boundary counter-terms
Sen:2008wa
;
Sen:2009wb
;
Sen:2009wc
. Hence, only the finite and cut-off independent term proportional to
-2\pi
contributes to the quantum entropy of extremal black holes and
\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}
.
and
\mathcal{G}(\theta)
is some function with the coordinates independent of the AdS
2
part,

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}(\theta)=({\sqrt{\det\bar{g}}})_{\text{ENH}}/\mathcal{G}(\tilde{r},\tau)=\frac{\ell^{2}\ell_{2}^{2}}{(\ell^{2}-a^{2})}\left(a^{2}\cos^{2}+r_{0}^{2}\right)\sin\theta.

(107)

Any regularization process never affects the cut-off independent part of the bulk contribution. Hence, the formula (
106
) always equips an unambiguous and natural correction result for extremal black hole entropy. Finally, we employ the
a_{4}(x)
form (
77
) in the formula (
106
) where we require to integrate the
W_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}W^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}
,
E_{4}
and
R^{2}
invariants around the ENH background (
4.3
) modulated by the function
\mathcal{G}(\theta)
(see
Appendix

C
for details). The final
\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}
results are obtained in terms of the parameters
\{\ell_{2},\,r_{0},\,\ell\}
and found to match exactly with the formulas (
4.3
) and (
101
) derived via taking
\beta\to\infty
in the non-extremal full horizon geometry analysis.

Black Hole Backgrounds

\bm{\mathcal{C}_{\text{zm}}}
