Appendix B
Holographic renormalization for AdS
4
black holes

In this section, we want to provide some essential details of the holographic boundary counterterm (
90
) and the related regularization process in obtaining the curvature invariant integration results (
91
).

With the Euclidean continuation (
t\to-i\tau
), the boundary geometry of AdS
4
background (
4.2
) at
r=r_{c}
is given by

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}s^{2}=\gamma_{\mu\nu}\mathrm{d}y^{\mu}\mathrm{d}y^{\nu}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\Delta_{r}}{{\rho_{c}}^{2}}\left(i\mathrm{d}\tau+\frac{a\sin^{2}\theta}{\Xi}\mathrm{d}\phi\right)^{2}+\frac{{\rho_{c}}^{2}}{\Delta_{\theta}}\mathrm{d}\theta^{2}+\frac{\Delta_{\theta}\sin^{2}\theta}{{\rho_{c}}^{2}}\left(ia\,\mathrm{d}\tau+\frac{{r_{c}}^{2}+a^{2}}{\Xi}\mathrm{d}\phi\right)^{2},

(220)

where the boundary parameters
\Delta_{r_{c}}
,
\Delta_{\theta}
,
\rho_{c}
, and
\Xi
are defined as

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}\Delta_{r_{c}}=({r_{c}}^{2}+a^{2})\bigg(1+\frac{{r_{c}}^{2}}{\ell^{2}}\bigg)-2m{r_{c}}+q^{2}+p^{2},\\
\Delta_{\theta}=1-\frac{a^{2}}{\ell^{2}}\cos^{2}\theta,\quad{\rho_{c}}^{2}={r_{c}}^{2}+a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta,\quad\Xi=1-\frac{a^{2}}{\ell^{2}}.\end{gathered}

Then, we can write the following forms for the determinant of metric
\gamma_{\mu\nu}
describing the boundary geometry and related Ricci scalar
\mathcal{R}

\displaystyle\begin{split}\det\gamma&=\frac{1}{\Xi^{2}}\left[\left({r_{c}}^{2}+a^{2}\right)\left(\frac{{r_{c}}^{2}}{\ell^{2}}+1\right)-2mr_{c}+q^{2}+p^{2}\right]\left({r_{c}}^{2}+a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta\right)\sin^{2}\theta,\\[3.0pt]
\mathcal{R}&=\frac{2}{\left({r_{c}}^{2}+a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta\right)^{3}}\bigg[{r_{c}}^{4}+\left({r_{c}}^{2}-2m{r_{c}}+q^{2}+p^{2}\right)a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\frac{a^{2}}{\ell^{2}}\Big({r_{c}}^{2}\left(1-5\cos^{2}\theta\right)-3a^{2}\cos^{4}\theta\Big)\left({r_{c}}^{2}+a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta\right)\bigg].\end{split}

(223)

With the above boundary setup, we can now express and expand the holographic boundary counterterm as

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{\text{HCT}}

\displaystyle=\int_{\partial(\text{AdS${}_{4}$})}\mathrm{d}^{3}y\sqrt{\det\gamma}\left(c_{1}+c_{2}\mathcal{R}\right)

(224)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\beta}\mathrm{d}\tau\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\phi\int_{0}^{\pi}\mathrm{d}\theta\sqrt{\det\gamma}\left(c_{1}+c_{2}\mathcal{R}\right)

(225)

\displaystyle=\frac{4\pi\beta}{\Xi\ell}\bigg[c_{1}{r_{c}}^{3}+\frac{1}{6\ell^{2}}\Big(c_{1}\left(4a^{2}+3\ell^{2}\right)\ell^{2}+4c_{2}\left(3\ell^{2}-2a^{2}\right)\Big)r_{c}-c_{1}m\ell^{2}\bigg]+\mathcal{O}\left({r_{c}}^{-1}\right).

(226)

Throughout this paper, we have added the above form of boundary term to the local part of logarithmic correction (i.e., the bulk contribution) and then extracted a finite and renormalized
\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}
contribution to AdS
4
black hole entropy in the limit
r_{c}\to\infty
,

Integrated AdS
4
Invariants

\bm{c_{1}}

\bm{c_{2}}

\frac{1}{16\pi^{2}}\int\mathrm{d}^{4}x\sqrt{\det\bar{g}}\,R_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}R^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}

-\dfrac{8}{\ell^{3}}

\dfrac{2}{\ell}

\frac{1}{16\pi^{2}}\int\mathrm{d}^{4}x\sqrt{\det\bar{g}}\,R_{\mu\nu}R^{\mu\nu}

-\dfrac{12}{\ell^{3}}

\dfrac{3}{\ell}

\frac{1}{16\pi^{2}}\int\mathrm{d}^{4}x\sqrt{\det\bar{g}}\,R^{2}

-\dfrac{48}{\ell^{3}}

\dfrac{12}{\ell}

\frac{1}{16\pi^{2}}\int\mathrm{d}^{4}x\sqrt{\det\bar{g}}\,W_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}W^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}

0

0

\frac{1}{16\pi^{2}}\int\mathrm{d}^{4}x\sqrt{\det\bar{g}}\,E_{4}

-\dfrac{8}{\ell^{3}}

\dfrac{2}{\ell}

Table 4:
Holographic renormalization data
(c_{1},c_{2})
for different integrated background invariants around the AdS
4
black holes for finite
\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}
contributions.

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}=\lim_{r_{c}\to\infty}\left[\frac{1}{16\pi^{2}}\int_{0}^{\beta}\mathrm{d}\tau\int_{r_{+}}^{r_{c}}\mathrm{d}r\int_{0}^{\pi}\mathrm{d}\theta\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\phi\,\sqrt{\det\bar{g}}\,a_{4}(x)+\mathcal{C}_{\text{HCT}}\right].

(227)

During this holographic renormalization process, the boundary counterterm (
226
) is found to be canceling the only and explicit bulk divergent terms involving
{r_{c}}^{3}
and
r_{c}
for the appropriate choice of
c_{1}
and
c_{2}
coefficients in the limit
r_{c}\to\infty
. For our specific aim of EMD-AdS theory, we have always used the typical
a_{4}(x)
form (
77
) or the
\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}
formula (
80
). Hence, it is also convenient to find the regulated results of integrated
W_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}W^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}
,
E_{4}
and
R^{2}
invariants via the same renormalization process (
227
). The relevant relations are presented in (
91
) for a generic charged and rotating background, where the associated
c_{1}
and
c_{2}
values are listed in
Table

4
. These data are common for the integrations over all Kerr-AdS (
q,p=0
), Reissner-Nordström-AdS (
a=0
), and Schwarzschild-AdS (
q,p,a=0
) backgrounds. Note that there is no such divergence due to infinite AdS boundary while integrating the curvature invariants around asymptotically-flat black holes (
\ell\to\infty
), and thus, we do not need any renormalization for them.

Finally, we want to mention the explicit forms of the integrated curvature invariants that are regulated via the mentioned holographic renormalization process and directly associated with the
\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}
formulas derived in
eqs.

93
and
4.3
. The contributions of Weyl tensor square
W^{2}

(=W_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}W^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma})
and Ricci scalar square
R^{2}
are listed as

\displaystyle\frac{1}{16\pi^{2}}\int_{\text{Sch-AdS}}\hskip-21.68121pt\mathrm{d}^{4}x\sqrt{\det\bar{g}}\,R^{2}=\frac{24r_{+}^{2}\left(\ell^{2}-r_{+}^{2}\right)}{\ell^{2}\left(\ell^{2}+3r_{+}^{2}\right)},

(228)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{16\pi^{2}}\int_{\text{Sch-AdS}}\hskip-21.68121pt\mathrm{d}^{4}x\sqrt{\det\bar{g}}\,W^{2}=\frac{4\left(\ell^{2}+r_{+}^{2}\right)^{2}}{\ell^{2}\left(\ell^{2}+3r_{+}^{2}\right)},

(229)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{16\pi^{2}}\int_{\text{RN-AdS}}\hskip-20.2355pt\mathrm{d}^{4}x\sqrt{\det\bar{g}}\,R^{2}=-\frac{24r_{+}^{2}}{\ell^{2}}+\frac{12r_{+}\left(r_{+}^{2}+\ell^{2}\right)}{\pi\ell^{4}}\beta,

(230)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{16\pi^{2}}\int_{\text{RN-AdS}}\hskip-20.2355pt\mathrm{d}^{4}x\sqrt{\det\bar{g}}\,W^{2}=\frac{4}{5}-\frac{28r_{+}^{2}}{5\ell^{2}}+\frac{32\pi r_{+}}{5\beta}+\frac{2\left(\ell^{4}+\ell^{2}r_{+}^{2}+4r_{+}^{4}\right)}{5\pi\ell^{4}r_{+}}\beta,

(231)

\displaystyle\lim_{\beta\to\infty}\frac{1}{16\pi^{2}}\int_{\text{RN-AdS}}\hskip-20.2355pt\mathrm{d}^{4}x\sqrt{\det\bar{g}}\,R^{2}=4-\frac{2r_{0}^{2}}{\ell_{2}^{2}}-\frac{2\ell_{2}^{2}}{r_{0}^{2}},

(232)

\displaystyle\lim_{\beta\to\infty}\frac{1}{16\pi^{2}}\int_{\text{RN-AdS}}\hskip-20.2355pt\mathrm{d}^{4}x\sqrt{\det\bar{g}}\,W^{2}=-\frac{2\left(\ell_{2}^{2}-r_{0}^{2}\right){}^{2}}{3r_{0}^{2}\ell_{2}^{2}},

(233)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{16\pi^{2}}\int_{\text{Kerr-AdS}}\hskip-23.12692pt\mathrm{d}^{4}x\sqrt{\det\bar{g}}\,R^{2}=\frac{6\beta\left(a^{2}+r_{+}^{2}\right)\left(\ell^{2}-r_{+}^{2}\right)}{\pi r_{+}\ell^{2}\left(\ell^{2}-a^{2}\right)},

(234)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{16\pi^{2}}\int_{\text{Kerr-AdS}}\hskip-23.12692pt\mathrm{d}^{4}x\sqrt{\det\bar{g}}\,W^{2}=\frac{\beta\left(r_{+}^{2}-a^{2}\right)\left(r_{+}^{2}+\ell^{2}\right)^{2}}{\pi r_{+}\ell^{2}\left(\ell^{2}-a^{2}\right)\left(a^{2}+r_{+}^{2}\right)},

(235)

\displaystyle\lim_{\beta\to\infty}\frac{1}{16\pi^{2}}\int_{\text{Kerr-AdS}}\hskip-23.12692pt\mathrm{d}^{4}x\sqrt{\det\bar{g}}\,R^{2}=\frac{12\left(a^{2}+r_{0}^{2}\right)\left(a^{2}+3r_{0}^{2}-\ell^{2}\right)}{\left(a^{2}-\ell^{2}\right)\left(a^{2}+6r_{0}^{2}+\ell^{2}\right)},

(236)

\displaystyle\lim_{\beta\to\infty}\frac{1}{16\pi^{2}}\int_{\text{Kerr-AdS}}\hskip-23.12692pt\mathrm{d}^{4}x\sqrt{\det\bar{g}}\,W^{2}=\frac{2\left(r_{0}^{2}+\ell^{2}\right)\Big(a^{4}\left(\ell^{2}-3r_{0}^{2}\right)+4a^{2}r_{0}^{2}\left(r_{0}^{2}+\ell^{2}\right)-r_{0}^{4}\ell^{2}+3r_{0}^{6}\Big)}{r_{0}^{2}\ell^{2}\left(a^{2}+r_{0}^{2}\right)\left(a^{2}+6r_{0}^{2}+\ell^{2}\right)}.

(237)

However, the contribution of integrated Euler density
E_{4}
is the same for all the cases,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{16\pi^{2}}\int_{\text{Sch/RN/Kerr-AdS}}\hskip-36.135pt\mathrm{d}^{4}x\sqrt{\det\bar{g}}\,E_{4}

\displaystyle=4.

(238)

This is justified because the integration of
E_{4}
over all the extremal and non-extremal black hole backgrounds defines the 4D Euler characteristics (
92
), which is a fixed number, i.e.,
\chi=2
.

Appendix C
Curvature invariants of AdS
4
extremal-near-horizon background

In this section, we list out the explicit forms of the curvature invariants for extremal near-horizon (ENH) backgrounds. These are crucial when turn to our analysis via the quantum entropy function formalism for computing
\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}
contributions to extremal black hole entropy (see
section

4.3
). The four-derivative invariants
R^{2}
,
E_{4}
and
W^{2}=W_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}W^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}
associated with the ENH background (
4.3
) are expressed as

\displaystyle R^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{144}{\ell^{4}},

(239)

\displaystyle E_{4}

\displaystyle=\frac{8}{\ell^{4}\left(a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta+r_{0}^{2}\right)^{6}}\bigg[a^{4}\cos^{4}\theta\Big(3a^{4}\left(a^{4}\cos^{4}\theta+6a^{2}r_{0}^{2}\cos^{2}\theta+15r_{0}^{4}\right)\cos^{4}\theta

\displaystyle\quad+5\big\{a^{4}\left(10r_{0}^{2}\ell^{2}+7r_{0}^{4}+\ell^{4}\right)+2a^{2}\left(5r_{0}^{2}\ell^{4}+16r_{0}^{4}\ell^{2}+9r_{0}^{6}\right)+r_{0}^{4}\left(7\ell^{4}+18r_{0}^{2}\ell^{2}+18r_{0}^{4}\right)\big\}

\displaystyle\quad-6a^{2}r_{0}^{2}\big\{a^{4}+2a^{2}\left(2r_{0}^{2}+\ell^{2}\right)+4r_{0}^{2}\ell^{2}-6r_{0}^{4}+\ell^{4}\big\}\cos^{2}\theta\Big)-2a^{2}r_{0}^{2}\Big(a^{4}\left(22r_{0}^{2}\ell^{2}+4r_{0}^{4}+19\ell^{4}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+2a^{2}r_{0}^{2}\left(11\ell^{4}+7r_{0}^{2}\ell^{2}-3r_{0}^{4}\right)+2r_{0}^{4}\left(2\ell^{4}-3r_{0}^{2}\ell^{2}-9r_{0}^{4}\right)\Big)\cos^{2}\theta

\displaystyle\quad-r_{0}^{4}\Big(a^{4}\left(r_{0}^{4}-2r_{0}^{2}\ell^{2}-5\ell^{4}\right)+a^{2}r_{0}^{2}\left(8r_{0}^{2}\ell^{2}+6r_{0}^{4}-2\ell^{4}\right)+r_{0}^{4}\ell^{2}\left(6r_{0}^{2}+\ell^{2}\right)\Big)\bigg],

(240)

\displaystyle W^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{48}{\ell^{4}\left(a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta+r_{0}^{2}\right)^{6}}\bigg[r_{0}^{4}\left(r_{0}^{4}-a^{2}\ell^{2}\right)^{2}+a^{8}\ell^{4}\cos^{4}\theta-a^{2}r_{0}^{2}\Big\{a^{4}\left(a^{2}+2r_{0}^{2}+\ell^{2}\right)^{2}\cos^{4}\theta

\displaystyle\quad-a^{2}\Big(16r_{0}^{4}\left(a^{2}+\ell^{2}\right)+8a^{2}\ell^{2}\left(a^{2}+\ell^{2}\right)+2r_{0}^{2}\left(3a^{4}+13a^{2}\ell^{2}+3\ell^{4}\right)+9r_{0}^{6}\Big)\cos^{2}\theta

\displaystyle+6a^{4}\ell^{4}-4r_{0}^{6}\left(a^{2}+\ell^{2}\right)+8a^{2}r_{0}^{2}\ell^{2}\left(a^{2}+\ell^{2}\right)+r_{0}^{4}\left(a^{4}+6a^{2}\ell^{2}+\ell^{4}\right)-6r_{0}^{8}\Big\}\cos^{2}\theta\bigg].

(241)

As mentioned in
section

4.3
, integration of the above near-horizon extreme invariants are found to be exactly in the same form as the full geometry extreme results (
4.3
) but differ by a factor of
-2\pi
due to the removal of divergence in the AdS
2
part of ENH geometry,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\lim_{\beta\to\infty}\int_{\text{full geometry}}\hskip-21.68121pt\mathrm{d}^{4}x\sqrt{\det\bar{g}}\,R^{2}&=-2\pi\int_{\text{ENH}}\mathrm{d}\theta\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{\phi}\,\mathcal{G}(\theta)\,R^{2},\\[5.0pt]
\lim_{\beta\to\infty}\int_{\text{full geometry}}\hskip-21.68121pt\mathrm{d}^{4}x\sqrt{\det\bar{g}}\,E_{4}&=-2\pi\int_{\text{ENH}}\mathrm{d}\theta\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{\phi}\,\mathcal{G}(\theta)\,E_{4},\\[5.0pt]
\lim_{\beta\to\infty}\int_{\text{full geometry}}\hskip-21.68121pt\mathrm{d}^{4}x\sqrt{\det\bar{g}}\,W^{2}&=-2\pi\int_{\text{ENH}}\mathrm{d}\theta\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{\phi}\,\mathcal{G}(\theta)\,W^{2},\end{split}

(242)

where the ENH function
\mathcal{G}(\theta)=\frac{\ell^{2}\ell_{2}^{2}}{(\ell^{2}-a^{2})}\left(a^{2}\cos^{2}+r_{0}^{2}\right)\sin\theta
must be operated in the range
0\leq\theta\leq\pi
and
0\leq\tilde{\phi}\leq 2\pi
. Here note that, we have obtained exactly similar relations also in the flat-space limit
\ell\to\infty
.

Appendix D
Logarithmic correction formulas for arbitrary dilaton coupling
\kappa

This section presents the general logarithmic correction formulas for each black hole embedded in the
U(1)
-charged EMD-AdS and EMD models with an arbitrary dilaton coupling constant
\kappa
. For that, one can utilize the trace anomaly or central charge data (
79
) in the
\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}
formulas derived in
eqs.

93
,
94
,
4.3
and
101
. In addition, the
\mathcal{C}_{\text{zm}}
data recorded in
Table

3
is found to be useful. In terms of a general
\kappa
parameter, the logarithmic correction formulas for the extremal and non-extremal Schwarzschild-AdS, Reissner-Nordström-AdS, and Kerr-AdS black holes are obtained as

\displaystyle\Delta S_{\text{BH}}^{\text{(Sch-AdS)}}

\displaystyle=\Bigg[-\frac{139}{36}+\kappa^{2}-\frac{\kappa^{4}}{6}+\frac{1}{\left(3r_{+}^{2}+\ell^{2}\right)\ell^{2}}\bigg\{\frac{35}{4}r_{+}^{4}+\left(\frac{\kappa^{4}}{2}-3\kappa^{2}+5\right)\ell^{2}r_{+}^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+\left(\frac{\kappa^{4}}{6}-\kappa^{2}+\frac{55}{12}\right)\ell^{4}\bigg\}\Bigg]\ln\mathcal{A}_{H},

(243)

\displaystyle\Delta S_{\text{BH}}^{\text{(RN-AdS)}}

\displaystyle=\Bigg[-\frac{53}{18}+\frac{4}{5}\kappa^{2}-\frac{2}{15}\kappa^{4}-\frac{2}{5\pi\ell^{4}r_{+}}\bigg\{\left(\kappa^{4}-6\kappa^{2}+\frac{45}{8}\right)\pi\ell^{2}r_{+}^{3}

\displaystyle\qquad-8\pi^{2}\left(\frac{\kappa^{4}}{12}-\frac{\kappa^{2}}{2}+\frac{55}{24}\right)\frac{\ell^{4}r_{+}^{2}}{\beta}-\frac{\beta}{2}\bigg(\left(\frac{\kappa^{4}}{2}-3\kappa^{2}-\frac{65}{8}\right)\ell^{2}r_{+}^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+\left(\frac{3}{4}\kappa^{4}-\frac{9}{2}\kappa^{2}-\frac{5}{4}\right)r_{+}^{4}+\left(\frac{\kappa^{4}}{12}-\frac{\kappa^{2}}{2}+\frac{55}{24}\right)\ell^{4}\bigg)\bigg\}\Bigg]\ln\mathcal{A}_{H},

(244)

\displaystyle\Delta S_{\text{BH}}^{\text{(Kerr-AdS)}}

\displaystyle=\Bigg[-\frac{103}{36}+\kappa^{2}-\frac{\kappa^{4}}{6}+\frac{\beta}{2\pi\ell^{2}\left(r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}\right)\left(\ell^{2}-a^{2}\right)r_{+}}\bigg\{

\displaystyle\qquad\left(\frac{\kappa^{4}}{12}-\frac{\kappa^{2}}{2}-\frac{25}{12}\right)a^{4}\ell^{2}-\left(\frac{\kappa^{4}}{12}-\frac{\kappa^{2}}{2}+\frac{55}{24}\right)a^{2}\ell^{4}+\frac{35}{8}r_{+}^{6}

\displaystyle\qquad+\bigg(\left(\frac{\kappa^{4}}{4}-\frac{3}{2}\kappa^{2}+\frac{5}{2}\right)\ell^{2}-\left(\frac{\kappa^{4}}{4}-\frac{3}{2}\kappa^{2}-\frac{15}{8}\right)a^{2}\bigg)r_{+}^{4}

\displaystyle\quad+\bigg(\left(\frac{\kappa^{4}}{12}-\frac{\kappa^{2}}{2}+\frac{55}{24}\right)\ell^{4}-\frac{35}{4}a^{2}\ell^{2}-\left(\frac{\kappa^{4}}{12}-\frac{\kappa^{2}}{2}-\frac{25}{12}\right)a^{4}\bigg)r_{+}^{2}\bigg\}\Bigg]\ln\mathcal{A}_{H},

(245)

\displaystyle\Delta S_{\text{BH}}^{\text{(ext,RN-AdS)}}

\displaystyle=\Bigg[-\frac{163}{36}+\frac{\kappa^{2}}{2}-\frac{\kappa^{4}}{12}-\left(\frac{\kappa^{4}}{24}-\frac{\kappa^{2}}{4}+\frac{5}{12}\right)\frac{\left(\ell_{2}^{4}+r_{0}^{4}\right)}{r_{0}^{2}\ell_{2}^{2}}\Bigg]\ln\mathcal{A}_{H},

(246)

\displaystyle\Delta S_{\text{BH}}^{\text{(ext,Kerr-AdS)}}

\displaystyle=\Bigg[-\frac{157}{36}+\kappa^{2}-\frac{\kappa^{4}}{6}+\frac{1}{r_{0}^{2}\left(\ell^{2}-a^{2}\right)\left(a^{2}+r_{0}^{2}\right)\left(a^{2}+6r_{0}^{2}+\ell^{2}\right)}\bigg\{

\displaystyle\qquad\left(\frac{\kappa^{4}}{12}-\frac{\kappa^{2}}{2}+\frac{55}{24}\right)a^{4}\ell^{4}+\frac{105}{8}r_{0}^{8}+\bigg(\left(\frac{95}{4}+\frac{3}{2}\kappa^{2}-\frac{\kappa^{4}}{4}\right)a^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\frac{\kappa^{4}}{4}-\frac{3}{2}\kappa^{2}+\frac{5}{2}\right)\ell^{2}\bigg)r_{0}^{6}+\bigg(\left(\frac{5}{6}\kappa^{4}-5\kappa^{2}+\frac{85}{6}\right)a^{2}\ell^{2}

\displaystyle\quad-\left(\frac{2}{3}\kappa^{4}-4\kappa^{2}-\frac{85}{24}\right)a^{4}-\left(\frac{\kappa^{4}}{12}-\frac{\kappa^{2}}{2}+\frac{55}{24}\right)\ell^{4}\bigg)r_{0}^{4}

\displaystyle\quad+\bigg(\left(\frac{\kappa^{4}}{3}-2\kappa^{2}+\frac{55}{6}\right)a^{2}\ell^{4}-\left(\frac{\kappa^{4}}{12}-\frac{\kappa^{2}}{2}+\frac{20}{3}\right)a^{4}\ell^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad-\left(\frac{\kappa^{4}}{12}-\frac{\kappa^{2}}{2}-\frac{25}{12}\right)a^{6}\bigg)r_{0}^{2}\bigg\}\Bigg]\ln\mathcal{A}_{H}.

(247)

In the flat-space limit (
\ell\to\infty
), the same formulas for the extremal and non-extremal Schwarzschild, Reissner-Nordström and Kerr black holes can be written as

\displaystyle\Delta S_{\text{BH}}^{\text{(Sch)}}

\displaystyle=\frac{13}{18}\ln\mathcal{A}_{H},

(248)

\displaystyle\Delta S_{\text{BH}}^{\text{(Kerr)}}

\displaystyle=\frac{31}{18}\ln\mathcal{A}_{H},

(249)

\displaystyle\Delta S_{\text{BH}}^{\text{(RN)}}

\displaystyle=\left[\frac{13}{18}+\left(\frac{\kappa^{4}}{60}-\frac{\kappa^{2}}{10}+\frac{11}{24}\right)\frac{\beta q_{e}^{4}}{\pi r_{+}^{5}}\right]\ln\mathcal{A}_{H},

(250)

\displaystyle\Delta S_{\text{BH}}^{\text{(ext,Kerr)}}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{9}\ln\mathcal{A}_{H},

(251)

\displaystyle\Delta S_{\text{BH}}^{\text{(ext,RN)}}

\displaystyle=\left[\kappa^{2}-\frac{\kappa^{4}}{6}-\frac{193}{36}\right]\ln\mathcal{A}_{H},

(252)

where the symbols and notations carry the same notion as depicted in
sections

4.2
and
4.3
. The above formulas appear to be crucial in understanding the quantum black holes in a wide range of EMD models characterizing the 4D description of different higher-dimensional gravity theories. In the case of EMD-AdS and EMD theories embedded into the low-energy string theory or supergravity models, one can set the appropriate dilaton coupling constant values, i.e.,
\kappa=1
,
\kappa=\sqrt{3}
, and
\kappa=\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}
into the relations to obtain the results mentioned in
section

4.4
.
